## 6. Variants: lactose-free, "dry curd", "whipped", "pressed", "Polish"

| Variant | What the tier (a) record says | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **Lactose-free** | The CFIA dairy page says: "When lactase is not permitted in standardized dairy products … The addition of lactase to these products is generally to remove the lactose content and will cause the food to deviate from the standard. If this is done, the common name must reflect the deviation … such as 'lactose-free milk' or 'lactose-reduced milk' [B.01.502(2)(d), FDR]." The summarising tool reports that Health Canada's List 5 does **not** list cottage cheese for lactase (s. 4, unverified). The CFIA claims guidance (https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/composition-and-quality, modified 2025-01-15): "lactose-free means that there is no detectable lactose in the food using an acceptable analytical method"; "lactose reduced … A significant reduction is considered to be a 25% reduction or more". **The company says** (Gay Lea FAQ, https://www.gaylea.com/faq/cottage-cheese): "Lactose Free dairy products are made lactose free by adding the lactase enzyme … to regular cottage cheese and sour cream." DFC lists "Nordica … Lactose Free Cottage Cheese 2% M.F." (s. 5.4). | Rule verified (CFIA). The lactase list row is unverified (tool). |
| **Dry curd** | No "dry curd cottage cheese" standard or definition appears in CFCS Vol. 7, CSI Vol. 1, the FDR or the SFCR (text search of the downloaded documents). The CDC page mentions "(creamed, lowfat or dry curd)" only in a nutrition sentence, which must not be used. A CFIA recall notice uses the product name "Cottage Cheese Dry Curd". That is a recall and goes to Appendix A only. | No Canadian standard found. U.S. has a separate federal standard (U.S. context only; not opened: UNVERIFIED). |
| **Whipped** | "Whipped" is a regulated descriptor only for butter and butter products (SFCR 246(a)(ii); CSI Vol. 1 s. 36). No cottage cheese provision uses it. Per the CFIA common-name guidance (s. 5.2), descriptive words may not appear *between* the words of a prescribed name. "Whipped cottage cheese" would therefore be read by that rule as a descriptor placed before the name. **This is our reading, not a CFIA statement.** | No Canadian standard found. |
| **Pressed / Polish cottage cheese** | Listed by DFC under Pine River and Wolski Dairy as "Pressed Cottage Cheese 0.8% M.F." and "Polish Cottage Cheese" (URL slugs). There is no separate standard. | Names seen; compliance not assessed. |
| **Flavoured ("Smooth Vanilla Bean", "Smooth Lemon")** | 7.4.5 names "fruits", "vegetables" and "relishes" as optional. It does not name other flavourings or sweeteners. DFC lists these Nordica Smooth items as "Cottage Cheese Product". | Name seen; do not infer. |

---

## 7. "Modified milk ingredients" / "milk-derived ingredients" (tier a)

- **FDR B.01.010(4)**, verbatim: "if a food contains ingredients of the same class, those ingredients may be shown by a class name if (a) they consist of more than one component and are not listed in the table to subsection B.01.009(1); and (b) their components are shown …". Class names come from the CFIA *Common Names for Ingredients and Components* (CNIC), which is incorporated by reference (FDR B.01.010(1)).
- **CNIC, current** (https://inspection.canada.ca/en/about-cfia/acts-and-regulations/list-acts-and-regulations/documents-incorporated-reference/common-names-ingredients-and-components, "Last amended: February 11, 2026", page modified 2026-05-27), Table 2:
  - item 7: "any of the following in liquid, concentrated, dry, frozen or reconstituted form, namely, butter, buttermilk, butter oil, milk fat, cream, milk, partly skimmed milk and skim milk" → **"milk ingredients"**
  - item 7.1: "any of the following in liquid, concentrated, dry, frozen or reconstituted form, namely, calcium reduced skim milk, cultured milk products, proteins derived from milk, ultrafiltered milk, whey, whey butter, whey cream and any other component of milk, except food additives, whether or not the component of milk has undergone additional processes" → **"milk-derived ingredients"**
  - item 7.2: "one or more ingredients or components set out in item 7 combined with any one or more ingredients or components set out in item 7.1" → **"milk-derived ingredients"**
  - item 9: "one or more species of bacteria" → **"bacterial culture"**
- **CNIC, archived version 2024-08-02 to 2024-12-18** (https://inspection.canada.ca/en/about-cfia/acts-and-regulations/list-acts-and-regulations/documents-incorporated-reference/archived-2024-12-common-names), item 7.1: "calcium reduced skim milk (obtained by the ion exchange process), casein, caseinates, cultured milk products, milk serum proteins, ultrafiltered milk, whey, whey butter, whey cream and any other component of milk the chemical state of which has been altered from that in which it is found in milk" → **"modified milk ingredients"**. *(The Dec 2024 to Feb 2026 archived version at https://inspection.canada.ca/node/6900 returned HTTP 404 today.)*
- **CFIA notice, 11 Feb 2026** (https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/notice-2026-02-11): "changing the common name 'modified milk ingredients' to 'milk-derived ingredients' and 'substances laitières modifiées' to 'ingrédients dérivés du lait'"; "industry will have until January 1, 2030 to update labels"; "During the transition period, regulated parties may comply with either the previous or new labelling requirements."
- **CFIA "What we heard"** (https://inspection.canada.ca/en/about-cfia/transparency/consultations-and-engagement/milk-ingredients/what-we-heard, modified 2026-02-12): the consultation ran "from August 22, 2025 to October 21, 2025"; "The CFIA received 60 responses, 46 from the online survey and 14 written responses by email". "Of those who did not agree with the proposal to change 'modified milk ingredients' to 'milk-derived ingredients,' most did not agree because consumers are familiar with the current names, and they felt these changes would reduce transparency and could mislead consumers." *(This is a CFIA summary of submissions, not a survey with tier (d) methodology.)*
- **Where MMI / milk-derived ingredients stand in cottage cheese:** CFCS 7.4.5 names "skim milk, evaporated skim milk or skim milk powder" as the base and "milk", "cream", "milk powder", "added lactose" as optional. **It does not name whey, caseinates, milk protein, ultrafiltered milk or any class name.** That is a statement of what the standard's text contains. It is **not** a finding about any product. Note also that CNIC item 7 places skim milk "in … dry … form" (skim milk powder) under "milk ingredients", not under "milk-derived ingredients". Separately, the CFIA dairy page says cultured skim milk (MicroGARD) "should not be included in the class name 'milk-derived ingredients'" and must be declared as "cultured skim milk" or "fermented skim milk" (https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/dairy).

---

## 8. Blue Cow logo, "100% Canadian milk" claims, Product/Made in Canada

### 8.1 DFC Blue Cow (Quality Milk) logo: owner's own site (tier a for what DFC says; it is not a government mark)
- **What the logo means, per DFC** (https://dairyfarmersofcanada.ca/en/blue-cow-logo/processors): "Products bearing the Dairy Farmers of Canada Quality Milk logo are made with 100% Canadian milk and milk ingredients." "The Blue Cow logo licence is free for processors and brand owners who demonstrate their products meet the certification criteria." "That's why we've made the logo our certification of origin."
- **Who may apply, per DFC** (https://dairyfarmersofcanada.ca/en/blue-cow-logo/processors/apply): "please fill out this application to begin the process of obtaining our royalty-free license agreement." Applicant types offered on the form are "Processor", "Brand Owner", "Food Service Provider", "Retailer" and "Advertising". The attestation text reads: "I am submitting a request for products made in Canada in which all dairy ingredients are Canadian in origin."
- **Consumer page** (https://dairyfarmersofcanada.ca/en/canadian-goodness/blue-cow-spotter/look-blue-cow-logo): "Our Blue Cow logo proudly represents products made with 100% Canadian milk and milk ingredients." *Other sentences on that page are health, safety or production claims. House rule: do not use them.*
- **Number of licensed products:** DFC does not publish a licence count on any page opened today. **Derived count (our arithmetic, not a DFC figure):** the Blue Cow Spotter directory (https://dairyfarmersofcanada.ca/en/canadian-goodness/blue-cow-spotter/search) showed 197 pages on 30 Sep 2026, with 20 listings on page 1 and 4 on the last page. That gives **3,924 product listings**. The "Cheese" filter (category 3541) gave **1,344 listings**, of which **31** have "cottage" in the URL slug:
  beatrice-cottage-cheese-2-mf-500g; island-farms-cottage-cheese-product-1-mf-500g; island-farms-cottage-cheese-product-1-mf-750g; island-farms-cottage-cheese-product-2-mf-500g; island-farms-cottage-cheese-product-2-mf-750g; island-farms-creamed-cottage-cheese-product-4-mf-500g; kirkland-signature-cottage-cheese-2-mf-2x500g; kirkland-signature-cottage-cheese-2-mf-3x750g; nordica-cottage-cheese-1-mf-4x113g; nordica-cottage-cheese-1-mf-500g; nordica-cottage-cheese-1-mf-750g; nordica-cottage-cheese-2-mf-250g; nordica-cottage-cheese-2-mf-500g; nordica-cottage-cheese-2-mf-750g; nordica-creamed-cottage-cheese-4-mf-500g; nordica-fat-free-cottage-cheese-500g; nordica-lactose-free-cottage-cheese-2-mf-450g; nordica-smooth-lemon-cottage-cheese-product-17-mf-4x100g; nordica-smooth-vanilla-bean-cottage-cheese-product-17-mf-4x100g; northumberland-cottage-cheese-1-mf-500g; northumberland-cottage-cheese-2-mf-500g; organic-meadow-organic-cottage-cheese-2-mf-500g; pine-river-farmers-cottage-cheese-500g; pine-river-polish-cottage-cheese-1-mf-750g; pine-river-pressed-cottage-cheese-08-mf-500g; sealtest-cottage-cheese-1-mf-250g; sealtest-cottage-cheese-1-mf-500g; sealtest-cottage-cheese-2-mf-500g; sealtest-creamed-cottage-cheese-4-mf-500g; wolski-dairy-polish-cottage-cheese-1-mf-750g; wolski-dairy-pressed-cottage-cheese-08-mf-500g.
  *These are URL slugs, not verbatim label names. A directory listing is not proof the logo is on a given package today. Absence from the directory is not proof a product lacks the logo.*

### 8.2 CFIA "100% Canadian milk" claim guidelines (tier a)
https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/dairy/canadian-milk (modified 2026-02-11)
- Scope: "This guideline applies only to the use of a '100% Canadian milk' claim or acceptable alternatives, with or without logos, vignettes etc., when used on dairy products."
- Criteria, verbatim: "All of the dairy ingredients in the product are derived from Canadian sources." "At least 1 of the following ingredients is present in the product in liquid, concentrated, dry, frozen or reconstituted form: (whole) milk; partly (partially) skimmed milk; skim milk; cream; condensed milk; buttermilk." "The ingredients listed above are to be listed separately in the ingredient list."
- "If the 'Made with 100% Canadian milk' claim is used, all 3 criteria listed above also apply. If the '100% Canadian dairy' claim is used, only criterion 1 listed above needs to be met."
- Background: "In October 2010, the Canadian Food Inspection Agency (CFIA) did public opinion research … consumers expect that all of the dairy ingredients are Canadian, with no imported dairy ingredients". *(CFIA-reported research. Its methodology is not on the page, so it is not tier (d).)*

### 8.3 CFIA "Product of Canada" / "Made in Canada" guidelines (tier a)
https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/origin-claims (modified 2023-12-06)
- "A food product may use the claim 'Product of Canada' when all or virtually all major ingredients, processing, and labour used to make the food product are Canadian."
- Minor ingredients: "Generally, the percentage referred to as very little or minor is considered to be less than a total of 2% of the product". This is the source of the popular "98%" description. **The current page does not use the figure "98%".**
- "The claim 'Canadian' is considered to be the same as a 'Product of Canada' claim".
- "A 'Made in Canada' claim with a qualifying statement can be used on a food product when the last substantial transformation of the product occurred in Canada … 'Made in Canada from domestic and imported ingredients' or 'Made in Canada from imported ingredients'."
- "When the claim '100% Canadian' is used on a label, the food or ingredient to which the claim applies must be entirely Canadian rather than 'all or virtually all' Canadian."
- **Dairy:** "Eggs from imported hens and milk from imported cows would qualify for the 'Product of Canada' claim provided that the hen laid its eggs in Canada, and the cow is milked in Canada."
- The guidelines are voluntary: "The use of 'Product of Canada' and 'Made in Canada' claims is voluntary. However, once a company chooses to make one of these claims, the product to which it is applied should meet these guidelines."

## 10. In-store checklist: what each label feature lawfully does and does not tell a viewer

| Label feature | What it lawfully tells you | What it does **not** tell you | Legal basis (tier a) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Common name "Cottage Cheese" or "Creamed Cottage Cheese"** (on the principal display panel) | The seller is using the prescribed name, which may only be used by a food that meets CFCS 7.4.5 / 7.4.6 (curd particles; skim milk / evaporated skim milk / skim milk powder base; bacterial cultures; ≤80% moisture; creamed ≥4% milk fat; only the listed optional ingredients plus additives Health Canada lists for cottage cheese). | Whether any regulator has tested this lot. Which optional ingredients or permitted additives were used. How much stabilizer is present. | FDR B.01.001(1) "common name", B.01.006(1), B.01.042; SFCR 201, 9; FDA s. 6 |
| **Any other name** ("cottage cheese product", "lactose free cottage cheese", "pressed cottage cheese", "(flavour) cottage cheese product") | The seller has **not** used the bare prescribed name. Under CFIA guidance, a modified standardized name must state "in all respects" how the food differs, and a non-standard food takes a descriptive name. | No regulation defines "cottage cheese product". The name alone does not tell you which way the product differs. Only the ingredient list gives clues, and it gives no amounts. | CFIA common-name and dairy pages (s. 5.2) |
| **Milk-fat % ("2% M.F.", "B.F.")** | Mandatory. It is the declared milk fat of this product. At or above 4% is required for "creamed cottage cheese". | Moisture (not required for cottage cheese). Origin of the milk. Whether stabilizers are present. *(House rule: state the % only as part of the product's name or grade. No fat-as-health commentary.)* | FDR B.08.002.3(k),(l); SFCR 248(a),(b) |
| **Ingredient list order** | Ingredients are "in descending order of their proportion by weight … determined before they are combined" (FDR B.01.008.2(3)(a)). | **Exception:** food additives, flavours, vitamins, mineral nutrients and seasonings up to 2% "may be shown at the end of the list of ingredients, in any order" (B.01.008.2(4)). An additive at the end is therefore not necessarily the smallest. Class names ("milk ingredients", "modified milk ingredients"/"milk-derived ingredients", "bacterial culture") group items. No quantities are given. | FDR B.01.008.2(3),(4); B.01.010; CNIC Table 2 |
| **Blue Cow (DFC Quality Milk) logo** | A DFC private certification-of-origin mark. **DFC says** it means "made with 100% Canadian milk and milk ingredients". The CFIA says any "100% Canadian milk" claim, "with or without logos", must be truthful and meet the CFIA criteria. | It is not a government mark. It says nothing about compliance with the cottage cheese composition standard, whether the food is "cottage cheese" or a "cottage cheese product", or the origin of non-dairy ingredients. | DFC processors/apply pages; CFIA "100% Canadian milk" guidelines |
| **"Product of Canada" / "Made in Canada from …"** (voluntary) | "Product of Canada": all or virtually all ingredients, processing and labour are Canadian (non-Canadian minor ingredients generally under 2% in total). For dairy, the cow is milked in Canada. "Made in Canada …" must carry a qualifier. | Nothing about composition or the standard. | CFIA origin-claims guidelines (modified 2023-12-06) |
| **"Product of [country]"** | Mandatory on imported prepackaged dairy products. | — | SFCR 250(1)(a) |
| **Name and principal place of business line** ("Manufactured for / by …", "Imported by/for …") | The person responsible for the food: either the one who "manufactured, prepared, produced, stored, packaged or labelled" it, or the one "for whom" it was. "Imported by/for" appears when the food is wholly made abroad and its origin is not otherwise shown. | For store brands ("for whom"), it need not name the actual manufacturer or plant. We found no federal requirement to print a dairy plant number on the consumer label (see UNVERIFIED). | SFCR 218(1)(b), 223; FDR B.01.007(1.1)(a); CFIA page https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-labels/labelling/industry/name-and-principal-place-business (modified 2022-07-06) |
| **Grade mark ("Canada 1")** | Not applicable. No Canadian grade exists for cottage cheese. | — | CFIA dairy page, grade names section |
| **Net quantity** | By weight (g) for consumer prepackaged cottage cheese. | — | Units of Measurement document, Table 2 item 6 |

**On-air framing that stays inside the rules:** "In Canada, 'cottage cheese' is a legally defined name. The federal standard, s. 7.4.5 of the CFIA's Canadian Food Compositional Standards, says what it is made from and what it may contain. If a tub says something other than 'cottage cheese' or 'creamed cottage cheese', that is the manufacturer's choice of name. The label and the standard are what we can compare. We are not saying any product breaks the rules."

<!-- ===== cottage_brands_national.md ===== -->

# Cottage cheese in Canada, 2026: dossiers on national and regional brands

**Compiled:** 30 Sep 2026. Every URL below was opened on 30 Sep 2026, between 01:05 and 01:45 UTC, unless the entry gives another time.
**Scope:** branded cottage cheese sold at Canadian retail in 2026. Store brands (No Name, Compliments, Great Value, Farm Boy, Kirkland Signature, Western Family, Lucerne, Selection) are listed where they turned up but get no dossier. Foremost gets a short entry because it looks like a regional dairy brand but is a Loblaw trademark.
**House rules applied:** This file contains no health, nutrition, protein, calorie, sodium, probiotic, additive-safety or food-safety commentary. Ingredient lists are copied verbatim and are not characterized. Where a company or retailer description mixes nutrition claims with other text, only the non-nutrition part is quoted and the cut is marked "[…]". Recalls appear only in Appendix A (DO NOT USE ON AIR). All prices come from the retailer's own site, API or flyer. Taste and texture wording appears only inside attributed quotes.
**Tiers:** (a) primary: government, company site, report or release, or the retailer's own product page, API or flyer. (b) Named outlet with byline and date. (c) Aggregator, flag only. (d) Named survey with methodology.
**Related file:** the federal definition of "cottage cheese" (CFCS 7.4.5 and 7.4.6) is covered in the sibling file `cottage_standards.md`. Section 0.3 re-quotes it from the CFIA page, opened here.

---

## 0. Headline findings

| # | Finding | Tier / source |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Sealtest's cottage cheese is labelled "Cottage Cheese Product", not "cottage cheese".** The front of the 2% tub reads "COTTAGE CHEESE PRODUCT / PRODUIT DE FROMAGE COTTAGE". sealtest.ca names all three SKUs "2% / 1% / 4% Cottage Cheese Product". | (a) Label image served by Loblaw's product API: https://digital.loblaws.ca/PCX/20092636002_EA/en/1/20092636002_en_front_800.png ; https://www.sealtest.ca/en/products/cottage-cheeses |
| 2 | **The Sealtest name belongs to Unilever PLC, not to Agropur.** The site says: "SEALTEST is a registered trademark of Unilever PLC. Used under licence in Canada by Agropur Cooperative." | (a) https://www.sealtest.ca/en/products/cottage-cheeses ; https://www.agropur.com/en/our-brands/sealtest |
| 3 | **Island Farms (Agropur, B.C.) is also labelled "Cottage Cheese Product"**, going by Dairy Farmers of Canada's (DFC) product directory and the label photo it hosts. The Island Farms website instead calls the product "2% Cottage Cheese". | (a) https://dairyfarmersofcanada.ca/en/canadian-goodness/blue-cow-spotter/island-farms-cottage-cheese-product-2-mf-500g ; https://www.islandfarms.com/en/products/cottage-cheeses/2-cottage-cheese |
| 4 | **Agropur's Amqui, Que. plant makes Sealtest cottage cheese.** A labour dispute there (a 24-hour strike on 12 Oct 2025, then a lockout until 21–24 Nov 2025) cut supply. Agropur's spokesperson said at the time that Amqui is the main plant in Canada making these cultured products. | (b) Radio-Canada, Alice Jacottin, 13 Oct 2025; La Voix de l'Est / Le Soleil, Patricia Rainville, 21 Nov 2025; CBC, Natalie Stechyson, 7 May 2026 |
| 5 | **Gay Lea (Nordica) is spending more than $200 million on its Clayson Road plant in Toronto.** Gay Lea says the project will "help address the national cottage cheese shortage". Completion is due in 2028, with up to 75 jobs. | (a) Gay Lea release, 11 Aug 2026: https://www.gaylea.com/news/about-us/gay-lea-foods-invests-more-than-200-million-to-help-shape-the-future-of-canadian-dairy |
| 6 | **Organic Meadow cottage cheese is discontinued.** The company told CBC in May 2026 that it dropped the product "last September" (Sept 2025) because of "capacity constraints". Its product menu opened today has no cottage cheese. | (b) CBC, 7 May 2026; (a) https://www.organicmeadow.com/ |
| 7 | **Of the branded products found, two are foreign-owned:** Beatrice (Lactalis Canada, part of Lactalis Group, headquartered in Laval, France) and Liberté (sold by General Mills to Sodiaal, a French dairy co-operative; the sale closed by Jan 2025). The widely sold brands Nordica (Gay Lea co-op), Sealtest, Natrel, Island Farms and Northumberland (Agropur co-op), and Dairyland, Neilson, Baxter and Scotsburn (Saputo Inc., Montréal) are Canadian-headquartered. The Sealtest name itself is licensed from Unilever PLC. | (a) lactalis.ca; sodiaal.fr; agropur.com; saputo.com; (b) Food Business News, 29 Jan 2025 |
| 8 | **Saputo's cottage cheese labels do not carry DFC's Blue Cow logo** (Dairyland, Neilson, Baxter, Scotsburn). Retailer label images show Saputo's own "Made with Canadian Milk" maple-leaf/barn mark or a maple leaf instead. None of the four brands appears among the cottage entries in DFC's Blue Cow directory. Nordica, Sealtest, Beatrice, Island Farms, Northumberland, Organic Meadow, Pine River, Wolski Dairy and Kirkland Signature cottage cheeses are listed there. | (a) DFC Blue Cow Spotter crawl (s. 1.3); label images (s. 2) |
| 9 | **Neither Lactantia nor Natrel has a cottage cheese page.** Lactalis Canada's 2026 "Buy Canadian" guide lists **Beatrice** as its only cottage cheese brand. natrel.ca has no cottage cheese category, although a Natrel-branded "Artisan-Style" cottage cheese is sold on Voilà, listed there as "Sealtest By Natrel". | (a) https://www.lactalis.ca/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Lactalis-Canada-Buy-Canada-2026.pdf ; https://www.natrel.ca/en ; Voilà 635721EA |
| 10 | **Not found on any Canadian shelf searched today:** Good Culture, fairlife cottage cheese, Riviera, Harmony Organic, Kawartha Dairy and Stirling cottage cheese. See s. 3. | (a) searches in s. 1 |

### 0.3 The legal name the brands are measured against (tier a)

Canadian Food Compositional Standards, Volume 7 (page "Date modified: 2025-05-01"), opened today at https://inspection.canada.ca/en/about-cfia/acts-and-regulations/list-acts-and-regulations/documents-incorporated-reference/canadian-food-compositional-standards-0 :

> "7.4.5 Cottage Cheese is the product, in the form of discrete curd particles, prepared from skim milk, evaporated skim milk or skim milk powder and harmless acid-producing bacterial cultures; (a) contains not more than 80% moisture; and (b) may contain (i) milk, (ii) cream, (iii) milk powder, (iv) salt, (v) added lactose, (vi) relishes, (vii) fruits, and (viii) vegetables."

> "7.4.6 Creamed Cottage Cheese is cottage cheese containing cream or a mixture of cream with milk or skim milk, or both, in such quantity that the final product and contains (a) not less than 4% milk fat; and (b) not more than 80% moisture."

The CFCS text opened today does not define "cottage cheese product". This file reports only which name appears on each label. It makes **no** finding or allegation about compliance.

Product URLs on loblaws.ca, realcanadiansuperstore.ca and the other Loblaw domains are formed from the `link` field the API returns. The Loblaw web pages themselves were not opened (they return 403 to scripts). The data comes from the API.

---

## 2. Brand dossiers

Each dossier gives: owner; where made; line-up; ingredients (verbatim, main SKU); front name; Blue Cow; the company's own words; dated news from 2023 to 2026; reading.

### 2.1 Nordica (Gay Lea Foods Co-operative Ltd.)

- **Owner:** Gay Lea Foods Co-operative Limited. The company describes itself as "a leading Canadian dairy co-operative owned by approximately 1,200 dairy farmer members in Ontario and Manitoba. Founded in 1958 …" (release, 11 Aug 2026, https://www.gaylea.com/news/about-us/gay-lea-foods-invests-more-than-200-million-to-help-shape-the-future-of-canadian-dairy). Head office: "10 Carlson Court, Suite 100, Etobicoke, ON M9W 6L2" (https://www.gaylea.com/faq/cottage-cheese). The lid reads "®/MD REGISTERED TRADEMARK OF / MARQUE DÉPOSÉE DE : GAY LEA FOODS CO-OPERATIVE LTD., MISSISSAUGA, ONTARIO L4W 5B4" (Loblaw API label image, URL in s. 0.2).
- **Where made:** The 11 Aug 2026 release ties the cottage cheese expansion to "its Clayson Road dairy manufacturing facility in Toronto" and says the project "will help address the national cottage cheese shortage". The release does **not** say in so many words that today's Nordica is made at Clayson Road, so the current plant is UNVERIFIED. The lid shows a hexagon printed "441" whose meaning (possibly a plant code) is UNVERIFIED.
- **Line-up (company, https://www.gaylea.com/products/cottage-cheese/):** Nordica 4% (500 g); Nordica 2% (500 g, 750 g); Nordica 1% (500 g, 750 g). Retailers also list Fat Free 500 g, Lactose Free 2% 450 g and On-The-Go Plain 4×113 g (Loblaw API; Voilà). DFC lists Nordica Smooth Lemon and Vanilla Bean "Cottage Cheese Product" 4×100 g. No retailer page opened today listed the Smooth line.
- **Ingredients, Nordica 2% (verbatim):**
  - Company page (https://www.gaylea.com/products/cottage-cheese/, 30 Sep 2026): "Milk ingredients, salt, citric acid, guar gum, mono- and diglycerides, xanthan gum, locust bean gum, bacterial culture, microbial enzyme, carbon dioxide, Vitamin D3* *made with vitamin D fortified milk."
  - Retailer (Voilà, https://voila.ca/products/862016EA/details, 30 Sep 2026): "Milk Ingredients, Salt, Citric Acid, Guar Gum, Mono And Diglycerides, Xanthan Gum, Locust Bean Gum, Bacterial Culture, Microbial Enzyme, Carbon Dioxide, Vitamin D3*. *Made With Vitamin D Fortified Milk."
- **Front name:** "NORDICA COTTAGE CHEESE 2%" and "100% CANADIAN DAIRY" (Loblaw label image). The 4% is sold as "Creamed" (Loblaw title "Nordica Cottage Cheese, Creamed 4%"; DFC "Creamed Cottage Cheese 4% M.F.").
- **Blue Cow:** **Yes.** The label shows the cow logo reading "QUALITY MILK / LAIT DE QUALITÉ", and the lid reads "TM DAIRY FARMERS OF CANADA / MC LES PRODUCTEURS LAITIERS DU CANADA". DFC lists 10 Nordica cottage SKUs (s. 1.3).
- **Company's own words (quotable, attributed):**
  - "[…] Nordica 2% is our most popular cottage cheese. Enjoy it with fruit, in pasta, salads, and everyday meals." (https://www.gaylea.com/products/cottage-cheese/ ; the opening words are nutrition claims and are omitted.)
  - On how it is made: "The milk coagulates into a solid curd that is then cut into smaller curds. The curds are then mixed with a dressing" (https://www.gaylea.com/faq/cottage-cheese).
  - "We do not use rennet in our cottage cheese. Microbial enzymes serve the same function in our product as rennet however they are sourced from microorganisms rather than animals." (same FAQ)
- **Dated news, 2023–2026:**
  - 11 Aug 2026 (a): the company announces more than $200 million for Clayson Road, Toronto: "This investment will help address the national cottage cheese shortage", with completion "in 2028" and "up to 75 new positions". CEO Suzanna Dalrymple is quoted: "This investment reflects our confidence in the future of Canadian dairy and in Gay Lea Foods' role in helping shape it." (release URL above)
  - 7 May 2026 (b): CBC (Natalie Stechyson) quotes a Gay Lea spokesperson: "What was once considered a niche product is now one of the fastest-growing segments in Canadian grocery stores." The spokesperson adds that "availability may vary by region and retailer." https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/cottage-cheese-shortage-canada-9.7187233
- **Reading:** Meets the standard name "cottage cheese" on the front; Canadian farmer-owned co-op; Blue Cow logo on label and in DFC directory.

### 2.2 Sealtest (Agropur Cooperative; trademark licensed from Unilever PLC)

- **Owner and chain:** Made and sold by Agropur Cooperative (Longueuil, QC), a farmer-owned co-op founded 1938 in Granby with "nearly 2,700 dairy farmer members" (https://www.agropur.com/en/our-cooperative/our-members). The **trademark** is Unilever's: "* SEALTEST is a registered trademark of Unilever PLC. Used under licence in Canada by Agropur Cooperative." (https://www.sealtest.ca/en/products/cottage-cheeses ; also https://www.agropur.com/en/our-brands/sealtest). Agropur's 2025 Annual Report reports "consolidated sales totalled $8.9 billion" and "Our 7,000 employees" (PDF URL in s. 0.2).
- **Where made:** Amqui, Québec, per tier (b) reports:
  - CBC (7 May 2026) reports Sylvain Charlebois's statement: "a labour dispute last fall at the Agropur plant in Amqui, where they make Sealtest cottage cheese".
  - Radio-Canada (13 Oct 2025) reports Agropur spokesperson Guillaume Bérubé's statement: "l'approvisionnement en fromage cottage et en crème sure sera réduit puisque l'usine d'Amqui est la principale à fabriquer ces produits de culture au pays."
  - Radio-Canada (29 Oct 2025): "L'usine Natrel d'Amqui produit plus de 30 millions de litres de lait annuellement, étant spécialisée dans le fromage cottage et la crème sure."
  - The label images examined show the Agropur logo but no plant address. No tier (a) Agropur page opened today names the cottage plant.
- **Line-up (sealtest.ca):** "2% Cottage Cheese Product", "1% Cottage Cheese Product", "4% Cottage Cheese Product", each 500 g. DFC also lists 1% in 250 g. The site says: "This product is available in Ontario, Quebec".
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim):**
  - Company (https://www.sealtest.ca/en/products/cottage-cheeses/2-cottage-cheese-product): "Skim milk, Ultrafiltered skim milk, Cream, Sugars (lactose), Skim milk powder, Salt, Cultured skim milk, Bacterial culture, Calcium chloride, Chymosin B, Carrageenan, Guar gum, Mono- and diglycerides, Xanthan gum, Citric acid, Lactic acid, Carbon dioxide."
  - Retailer, Provigo via Loblaw API (`/products/20092636002_EA?…storeId=7297&banner=provigo`): "Skim Milk, Ultrafiltered Skim Milk, Cream, Sugars (lactose), Skim Milk Powder, Salt, Cultured Skim Milk, Bacterial Culture, Calcium Chloride, Chymosin B, Carrageenan, Guar Gum, Mono- And Diglycerides, Xanthan Gum, Citric Acid, Lactic Acid, Carbon Dioxide."
  - Retailer, Voilà (https://voila.ca/products/862555EA/details) shows a **different order**: "Skim milk, Cream, Ultrafiltered skim milk, Lactose, Skim milk powder, Salt, Guar gum, Mono and diglycerides, Xanthan gum, Carrageenan, Citric acid, Cultured skim milk, Bacterial culture, Lactic acid, Calcium chloride, Microbial enzyme, Carbon dioxide". The site carries its own disclaimer that labels may differ.
- **Front name:** "COTTAGE CHEESE PRODUCT / PRODUIT DE FROMAGE COTTAGE", with the side band "LOW FAT COTTAGE CHEESE PRODUCT" (Loblaw label image https://digital.loblaws.ca/PCX/20092636002_EA/en/1/20092636002_en_front_800.png). Voilà titles the product "Sealtest 2% Cottage Cheese Low Fat 500 g"; DFC titles it "Cottage Cheese 2% M.F.". **The retailer and DFC titles drop the word "Product" that appears on the label.**
- **Blue Cow:** **Yes.** The label (lid) shows "DAIRY FARMERS OF CANADA QUALITY MILK / LES PRODUCTEURS LAITIERS DU CANADA LAIT DE QUALITÉ", and DFC lists it.
- **Company's own words (retailer copy supplied under the brand, Voilà 862555EA):** "[…] Sealtest by Natrel cottage cheese is perfect to layer that scrumptious home made lasagna and it lends the fresh taste of milk and the richness of cheese to favourite desserts or snacks." The first sentence is a nutrition claim and is omitted.
- **Dated news, 2023–2026 (tier b; labour items labelled as reported):**
  - 12–13 Oct 2025: members of the Syndicat national du lait (CSD) begin a 24-hour strike at Amqui on Sunday 12 Oct at 19:00, and Agropur declares a lockout the next day. Radio-Canada, Alice Jacottin, 13 Oct 2025, https://ici.radio-canada.ca/nouvelle/2199307/greve-usine-lock-out-agropur-amqui . About 100 employees are affected. Agropur, via Bérubé, said grocers would face shortages of cottage cheese and sour cream "d'ici quelques jours". (Agropur's stated reasons for the lockout concern food safety and hygiene, so they are **not for air**; see Appendix A.)
  - 29 Oct 2025: Radio-Canada (Alice Jacottin) reports: "Agropur confirme que l'ensemble de la production de fromage de l'usine d'Amqui sera délocalisée, et qu'il y aura des pertes d'emplois." Per the CSD, 10–15 of about 100 workers were on the cheese line. The article also reports Bérubé's statement that "Agropur souhaite investir et augmenter sa production de fromage cottage." https://ici.radio-canada.ca/nouvelle/2203026/agropur-lockout-perte-emploi-fromage-cottage . **Careful: "production de fromage" (cheese) is reported separately from cottage cheese; do not say cottage production left Amqui.**
  - 21 Nov 2025: La Voix de l'Est / Le Soleil (Patricia Rainville) reports that workers accepted the employer's third and final offer by 70%, with a return to work "dès lundi". Bérubé is quoted: "Les consommateurs pourront donc retrouver nos produits de fromage cottage et de crème sure en épicerie dans les prochaines semaines." The new agreement runs 6½ years; the dispute began 12 Oct. https://www.lavoixdelest.ca/affaires/2025/11/21/fin-du-lock-out-chez-agropur-damqui-HYMKLBLM3JH73DV6ZZQJ23TYLM/
  - 3 Mar 2026: CTV News Montreal (Christine Long) reports: "Agropur, makers of Sealtest cottage cheese, told CTV News their plant is operating at full capacity and that they have plans to increase production." https://www.ctvnews.ca/montreal/article/got-cottage-cheese-montreal-shoppers-are-having-trouble-finding-it/
  - 15 Jul 2026 (a, context only, not cottage): Agropur announces an agreement to sell its fine cheese business (Oka, Saint-Hyacinthe plants) to Lactalis. https://www.agropur.com/en/news
- **Reading:** Sold as "cottage cheese product", not "cottage cheese" (front label; sealtest.ca). Canadian co-op maker (Agropur) using a brand licensed from Unilever PLC. Blue Cow logo.

### 2.3 Natrel "Artisan-Style" (listed on Voilà as "Sealtest By Natrel") (Agropur)

- **Owner:** Agropur Cooperative. Natrel is listed on https://www.agropur.com/en/our-brands .
- **Where made:** UNVERIFIED.
- **Line-up:** 2% and 1% "Artisan Style", 500 g (Voilà 635721EA, 635720EA). **natrel.ca shows no cottage cheese category:** https://www.natrel.ca/en/products/cottage-cheeses redirects to the homepage, and the nav lists milks, butters, creams, creamers, ice creams, mochis.
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim, Voilà https://voila.ca/products/635721EA/details):** "Skim milk, Cream, Ultrafiltered skim milk, Lactose, Skim milk powder, Salt, Guar gum, Mono and diglycerides, Xanthan gum, Carrageenan, citric acid, Cultured skim milk, Bacterial culture, Lactic acid, Calcium chloride, Microbial enzyme, Carbon dioxide."
- **Front name:** The Voilà image (https://voila.ca/images-v3/2d92d19c-0354-49c0-8a91-5260ed0bf531/d9b5cc89-7f12-4062-afc4-3ac8d61b90c4/500x500.jpg) reads "Natrel COTTAGE CHEESE 2% / ARTISAN-STYLE / LOW FAT COTTAGE CHEESE".
- **Blue Cow:** Not visible on the front image; no DFC cottage entry under "Natrel". "Sealtest By Natrel" is in DFC's brand list, category not checked. **UNVERIFIED.**
- **Company's own words:** Voilà uses the Sealtest text (s. 2.2).
- **Reading:** Front name is "cottage cheese" ("Low Fat Cottage Cheese"); Canadian co-op; Blue Cow status unverified.

### 2.4 Northumberland (Agropur)

- **Owner:** Agropur. "The Northumberland brand joined the Agropur family in 2014." It "hails from New Brunswick and is distributed throughout the Atlantic provinces." (https://www.agropur.com/en/our-brands/northumberland)
- **Where made:** UNVERIFIED. The Voilà listing says "Northumberland by Natrel is available in New Brunswick."
- **Line-up:** 1% and 2%, 500 g (Voilà; DFC).
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim, Voilà https://voila.ca/products/630639EA/details):** "Ingredients Skim milk, Ultrafiltered skim milk, Cream, Sugars (lactose), Skim milk powder, Salt, Cultured skim milk, Bacterial culture, Calcium chloride, Chymosin B, Carrageenan, Guar gum, Mono- and diglycerides, Xanthan gum, Citric acid, Lactic acid, Carbon dioxide." This is the same list, in the same order, as sealtest.ca's 2% page.
- **Front name:**
  - Current Voilà image (https://voila.ca/images-v3/2d92d19c-0354-49c0-8a91-5260ed0bf531/c0b4d99e-689e-4d5c-aef7-719a5417cb6a/500x500.jpg): "NORTHUMBERLAND BY Natrel / COTTAGE CHEESE 2% / … / COTTAGE CHEESE".
  - Older image on the DFC entry (https://dairyfarmersofcanada.ca/en/canadian-goodness/blue-cow-spotter/northumberland-cottage-cheese-2-mf-500g): "NORTHUMBERLAND / COTTAGE CHEESE PRODUCT 2%".
  - Voilà copy: "Same recipe, same great taste, new look."
- **Blue Cow:** Listed in DFC ("Quality Milk").
- **Company's own words (Voilà):** "Northumberland by Natrel is available in New Brunswick. Same recipe, same great taste, new look." The first sentence (nutrition) is omitted.
- **Dated news:** 23 Apr 2026 (b, context, not cottage-specific): CBC (Hannah Rudderham) reports that Agropur will close its Sussex-area NB plant and its Truro NS ingredients plant by the end of 2028, with 90 potential job losses, and will expand Bedford NS and Miramichi NB. The Sussex plant "is known for its preparation of butter and other dairy products, such as milk powders". https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/new-brunswick/agropur-dairy-plant-closure-9.7174615 . Do not link this to cottage cheese.
- **Reading:** Front name is now "cottage cheese" (the older label read "cottage cheese product"); Canadian co-op; Blue Cow (DFC).

### 2.5 Island Farms (Agropur)

- **Owner:** Agropur ("As part of the Agropur family, the largest dairy co-operative in Canada", https://www.islandfarms.com/en/about). Brand history dates to 1944 (same page).
- **Where made:** The company says it is "made from fresh B.C. milk" (https://www.islandfarms.com/en/products/cottage-cheeses). The plant is UNVERIFIED.
- **Line-up:** 1% and 2% cottage cheese, 500 g and 750 g (company site). DFC adds "Creamed Cottage Cheese Product 4% M.F." 500 g.
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim, company, https://www.islandfarms.com/en/products/cottage-cheeses/2-cottage-cheese):** "Skim milk, Milk, Skim milk powder, Salt, Cream, Cultured skim milk, Bacterial culture, Calcium chloride, Chymosin B, Guar gum, Locust bean gum, Mono- and diglycerides, Polysorbate 80, Xanthan gum."
- **Front name:** The label photo on DFC (https://dairyfarmersofcanada.ca/en/canadian-goodness/blue-cow-spotter/island-farms-cottage-cheese-product-2-mf-500g) reads "island FARMS / COTTAGE CHEESE PRODUCT 2%", and DFC names it "Cottage Cheese Product 2% M.F." The company site calls it "2% Cottage Cheese". **The two sources disagree; the label photo governs, but its date is unknown.**
- **Blue Cow:** Listed in DFC (5 SKUs).
- **Company's own words:** "Island Farms 2 % cottage cheese is made from fresh BC milk and offer larger and more distinct curds. So you can enjoy on fruit, baked potatoes or simply as is." (2% page; the opening phrase is a nutrition claim and is omitted.) Also, from the Agropur brand page: "Made right, right here."
- **Retail availability:** **No retailer page opened today (Save-On Langley, Loblaw BC stores, Voilà default) lists Island Farms cottage cheese.** A price is UNVERIFIED.
- **Reading:** Sold as "cottage cheese product" per DFC name and label photo; Canadian co-op; Blue Cow.

### 2.6 Dairyland (Saputo Inc.)

- **Owner:** Saputo Inc., Montréal-based and TSX-listed ("Montreal-based dairy producer Saputo Inc. SAP-T", Globe and Mail, 7 Aug 2026). Saputo says: "Dairyland - The #1 brand in Western Canada" and that the division sells "under customer brand names, as well as under the Company's own brands" (https://saputo.com/en/our-products/canada-sector). The acquisition year is UNVERIFIED (s. 0.2).
- **Where made:** Company heritage page: "Opened in 1964, our Burnaby dairy plant … Since then, we have built a state-of-the-art facility in Port Coquitlam" (https://www.dairyland.ca/en/our-heritage). **Which plant makes the cottage cheese is UNVERIFIED.** Dairyland's "Buy Canadian Dairy Guide" lists cottage cheese under "MADE IN CANADA OR PRODUCT OF CANADA" and says "Dairyland sources milk from Canadian dairy farms for all our products manufactured in Canada." (PDF, https://saputo.canto.com/direct/document/fi87550ib53ef433uvc5agpj63/uDeWWbtGgOqaX4JTDLLzN8JEERA/original?content-type=application%2Fpdf&name=Dairyland+Canadian+Dairy+Guide_EN.pdf)
- **Line-up (https://www.dairyland.ca/en/products/cottage-cheese):** Fat Free (500 g); Light (500 g, 750 g); Cottage Cheese [2%] (250 g, 500 g, 750 g); 4% (500 g); Dry Curd Fat Free (600 g).
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim):**
  - Company (https://www.dairyland.ca/en/products/cottage-cheese/2-cottage-cheese): "Milk ingredients, Salt, Locust bean gum, Guar gum, Mono and diglycerides, Carrageenan, Xanthan gum, Polysorbate 80, Bacterial culture, Microbial enzyme, Carbon dioxide (to maintain product freshness)."
  - Retailer, Save-On-Foods API sku 00068700463006: "Milk ingredients, Salt, Locust bean gum, Guar gum, Mono- and diglycerides, Carrageenan, Xanthan gum, Polysorbate 80, Bacterial culture, Microbial enzyme, Carbon dioxide (to maintain product freshness)."
  - Dry curd (Voilà 511646EA): "Skim milk, bacterial culture, microbial enzyme."
- **Front name:** "FROMAGE COTTAGE CHEESE" (2%, 750 g); "FROMAGE COTTAGE CHEESE / DRY CURD / CAILLÉ SEC" (600 g). Loblaw label images https://digital.loblaws.ca/PCX/20831067_EA/en/1/20831067_en_front_800.png and …/21672216_EA/en/1/21672216_en_front_800.png.
- **Blue Cow:** **Not established.** The retailer tile shows a white maple-leaf-and-barn symbol ® with "MADE WITH CANADIAN MILK" (Saputo's own mark), not the DFC cow. A small round blue badge on the lid is not legible. Dairyland has no DFC cottage entry. The website image alt text reads "Fresh milk from Canadian dairy farms" and "Made in Canada".
- **Company's own words:**
  - "Cottage cheese is made by separating fresh milk to produce skim milk, which is then transferred to cheese vats. A bacterial culture is added to acidify and gently coagulate the milk. … For our dressed cottage cheese, the washed curds are carefully blended with a thick, creamy dressing before packaging." (https://www.dairyland.ca/en/products/cottage-cheese)
  - "Enjoy the rich and creamy taste of our plain cottage cheese as the key ingredient in your favourite recipe." (retailer copy, Loblaw API 20831067_EA)
- **Survey claim (tier d candidate; methodology NOT obtained):** The website shows an award badge with alt text "#1 Most Trusted by Canadians in 2026 by BrandSpark Most Trusted Awards" and the text "In the Cottage Cheese category, based on the 2026 BrandSpark Canadian Shopper Study." Not usable until BrandSpark's methodology is opened; see UNVERIFIED.
- **Dated news:** 7 Aug 2026 (b): The Globe and Mail (Kate Helmore) reports that Saputo "plans to allocate capital to the processing of concentrated whey, cottage cheese and […] ready-to-drink products" (a product-category word is omitted) and to expand manufacturing "in Canada and the U.S." https://www.theglobeandmail.com/business/article-saputo-dairy-producer-earnings-protein-maxxing/ . (Saputo's own Q1 FY2027 release at newsroom.saputo.com timed out; the capacity figure for the Friendship, N.Y. plant is **U.S. context** and is not verified here.)
- **Reading:** Front name is "cottage cheese" (plus "dry curd" variant); Canadian-headquartered, TSX-listed Saputo; uses Saputo's own "Made with Canadian Milk" mark, not the DFC Blue Cow.

### 2.7 Neilson (Saputo Inc.)

- **Owner and chain:** "In 1947 the firm was acquired by George Weston … When Saputo acquired Neilson Dairy in 2008, it joined Canada's largest dairy producer." (https://www.neilsondairy.com/en/about-us). Saputo also says: "Neilson Dairy - The #1 brand in Ontario" (https://saputo.com/en/our-products/canada-sector).
- **Where made:** UNVERIFIED.
- **Line-up (https://www.neilsondairy.com/en/products/cottage-cheese):** "0.3% Fat Free Cottage Cheese" (500G); "1% Light Cottage Cheese" (500G); "2% Cottage Cheese" (500G).
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim, Voilà https://voila.ca/products/1452981EA/details):** "Milk ingredients, salt, guar gum, carrageenan, mono- and diglycerides, locust bean gum, xanthan gum, polysorbate 80, bacterial culture, microbial enzyme*, carbon dioxide (to maintain product freshness). *May be present. CONTAINS: milk." (The Loblaw API for 21261519_EA gives the same list.)
- **Front name:** "FROMAGE COTTAGE CHEESE", with "MADE WITH CANADIAN MILK / FAIT AVEC DU LAIT CANADIEN" and a red maple leaf (Loblaw image https://digital.loblaws.ca/PCX/21261519_EA/en/8/6680071273_enfr_front_noPlunge_ecomm_A_1_GS1_Ecommerce_800.png).
- **Blue Cow:** Not on the front image; no DFC cottage entry.
- **Company's own words:** "Made with Canadian milk, Neilson cottage cheese delivers the dependable quality families trust. It works well in simple snacks, pairs nicely with fruit, and blends smoothly into savoury dishes like salads, pastas, and baked recipes." (cottage page)
- **Reading:** Front name is "cottage cheese"; Saputo (Canadian-headquartered); maple-leaf "Made with Canadian milk" statement, not the Blue Cow.

### 2.8 Baxter (Saputo Inc.)

- **Owner and chain:** "In 2001, the Baxter brand was acquired by Saputo." The business began in 1924 in Saint John, NB (https://www.baxtermilk.ca/en/our-history). Site footer: "© Saputo Inc."
- **Where made:** UNVERIFIED.
- **Line-up (retailers):** 2% (500 g, 750 g); 1% Light (500 g, 750 g); Fat-Free 0.4% (500 g) (Voilà; Loblaw API; Giant Tiger).
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim):**
  - Voilà (https://voila.ca/products/76025EA/details): "Milk Ingredients, Salt, Locust Bean Gum, Guar Gum, Mono And Diglycerides, Carrageenan, Xanthan Gum, Polysorbate 80, Bacterial Culture, Microbial Enzyme, Carbon Dioxide (To Maintain Product Freshness)"
  - Loblaw API (20372576003_EA, Atlantic Superstore 0354) gives the same list.
- **Front name:** "Baxter COTTAGE CHEESE 2% M.F.", lid "FROMAGE COTTAGE CHEESE" (https://digital.loblaws.ca/PCX/20372576003_EA/en/1/20372576003_en_front_800.png).
- **Blue Cow:** No. The retailer tile shows Saputo's maple-leaf/barn "MADE WITH CANADIAN MILK" mark; no DFC cottage entry.
- **Company's own words (retailer copy, Loblaw API):** "[…] Proud to deliver the best quality New Brunswick milk for almost 100 years. At Baxter, we are committed to caring for our communities and have championed always fresh and local dairy products for our consumers since 1924."
- **Reading:** Front name is "cottage cheese"; Saputo; not the Blue Cow.

### 2.9 Scotsburn (Saputo Dairy Products Canada G.P., under licence)

- **Owner and chain:** "In 2014, Saputo took over the production of these products [milk, cream, buttermilk, cottage cheese, sour cream, butter, holiday nog]" (https://www.scotsburnmilk.ca/en/our-history). "SCOTSBURN is a registered trademark and is used under license by Saputo Dairy Products Canada G.P." (https://www.scotsburnmilk.ca/en/products). The licensor is not named. Agropur's brand list separately shows "Scotsburn joins Farmers", which DFC tags as an **ice cream** brand; it is unrelated to the cottage cheese.
- **Where made:** UNVERIFIED. The history page says only "facilities in Nova Scotia and Newfoundland" (historic).
- **Line-up:** 2% (500 g); 2% No Salt Added (500 g, per label image of Loblaw 20972853_EA); 1% Low Fat (500 g); Fat-Free 0.4% (500 g); 2% "Lasagna Style" (500 g) (Voilà; Loblaw API; Giant Tiger).
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim, Loblaw API 20317595002_EA, Atlantic Superstore Halifax):** "Milk Ingredients, Salt, Locust Bean Gum, Guar Gum, Vegetable Mono And Diglycerides, Carrageenan, Xanthan Gum, Polysorbate 80, Bacterial Culture, Microbial Enzyme, Carbon Dioxide." (The No Salt Added SKU 20972853_EA lists the same without "Salt"; this is a literal label difference only.)
- **Front name:** "SCOTSBURN Fromage Cottage Cheese 2% M.F./M.G." (https://digital.loblaws.ca/PCX/20972853_EA/en/1/20972853_en_front_800.png).
- **Blue Cow:** No. The tile shows Saputo's "MADE WITH CANADIAN MILK" mark, and there is no DFC cottage entry. The lid text "Made with milk from Canadian dairy farms" is partly legible.
- **Company's own words (retailer copy, Loblaw API):** "[…] Scotsburn has been delivering the best quality milk to Nova Scotia families for more than 100 years. Always fresh and local, Scotsburn has a long history of offering our customers 'nothing but the best' dairy products from Scotsburn, Nova Scotia."
- **Reading:** Front name is "cottage cheese"; made by Saputo under a trademark licence; not the Blue Cow.

### 2.10 Beatrice (Lactalis Canada Inc. / Lactalis Group, France)

- **Owner and chain:** Lactalis Canada: "part of Lactalis Group, the world's #1 dairy group, headquartered in Laval, France. Established in 1933 by André Besnier, Lactalis Group is a family-owned global business" (https://www.lactalis.ca/about-us/). The history timeline (https://www.lactalis.ca/about-us/our-history/) runs: 1997 "Parmalat Canada acquired these existing brands"; 2011 Lactalis acquires Parmalat; 2020 "Parmalat Canada becomes Lactalis Canada to reflect its full ownership by Lactalis Group." The 2026 Buy Canadian guide lists "Cottage Cheese — Beatrice" as Lactalis Canada's only cottage cheese (https://www.lactalis.ca/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Lactalis-Canada-Buy-Canada-2026.pdf). The label says "TRADEMARKS OWNED … UNDER LICENSE BY … TORONTO, ON", partly legible.
- **Where made:** UNVERIFIED. Lactalis says it has "19 manufacturing facilities located throughout Québec, Ontario, Manitoba, Alberta and British Columbia" (about page). Giant Tiger carries three regional Beatrice SKUs: 157871 tagged AB/MB/SK, 222701 tagged AB, and 454961 tagged QC.
- **Line-up:** 2% 500 g seen. Other fat levels are UNVERIFIED (beatrice.ca shows only a province selector).
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim, from the label panel images on Giant Tiger's product page https://www.gianttiger.com/products/beatrice-cottage-cheese-2-m-f-500-g-3, image https://cdn.shopify.com/s/files/1/0557/6858/0157/files/gs1_157871_gs1_15.jpg set):** "Ingredients: Milk ingredients, Salt, Guar gum, Carrageenan, Locust bean gum, Sugars (dextrose), Lactic acid, Microbial enzyme, Bacterial culture, Carbon dioxide. Contains: Milk." In French: "Ingrédients : Substances laitières, Sel, Gomme de guar, Carraghénine, Gomme de caroube, Sucres (dextrose), Acide lactique, Enzyme microbienne, Culture bactérienne, Dioxyde de carbone. Contient : Lait." **Caveat:** Giant Tiger served identical image files for all three Beatrice handles, so these images may not be specific to each regional SKU.
- **Front name:** "Beatrice® Cottage Cheese / Fromage cottage", "2% M.F." (same images).
- **Blue Cow:** **Yes.** The label shows "DAIRY FARMERS OF CANADA QUALITY MILK", and DFC lists Beatrice Cottage Cheese 2% M.F. 500g (UPC 055300132029).
- **Company's own words:** None retrieved; the Giant Tiger body text is empty and beatrice.ca requires a province and language selection.
- **Dated news (a, context):** Lactalis Canada announces it will acquire Agropur's fine cheese division (Oka, Monsieur Gustav, L'Extra, two plants) (https://www.lactalis.ca/ homepage; Agropur, 15 Jul 2026). Not cottage-related.
- **Reading:** Front name is "cottage cheese"; **foreign-owned (Lactalis Group, France)**; Blue Cow logo.

### 2.11 Liberté (Yoplait Liberté Canada Co. / Sodiaal, France)

- **Owner and chain:** Founded in Montréal in 1936 by the Kaporovsky family (https://www.liberte.ca/en/about-us). The website "is operated by Yoplait Liberté Canada Co." (https://www.liberte.ca/en/terms-of-use). General Mills' sale of its Canadian yogurt business, "including Yoplait and Liberte, as well as a manufacturing facility in Saint-Hyacinthe, Que.", to Sodiaal was reported complete on 29 Jan 2025 (Food Business News, Eric Schroeder, URL in s. 0.2). Sodiaal describes itself as a "Coopérative Laitière Française" (https://www.sodiaal.fr/en/). **The year General Mills acquired Liberté is UNVERIFIED here.**
- **Where made:** UNVERIFIED. The Saint-Hyacinthe plant in the FBN report is described as part of the yogurt business, not as the cottage cheese plant. The side panel on the retailer image names a Liberté entity, but the text is not legible.
- **Line-up:** 2% 500 g (Voilà). liberte.ca's product list is JS-rendered and showed no cottage cheese in the fetched HTML.
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim, Voilà https://voila.ca/products/207987EA/details):** "Skim milk, Milk, Evaporated skim milk, Skim milk powder, Cream, Salt, Citric acid, Guar gum, Mono and diglycerides, Xanthan gum, Locust bean gum, Bacterial culture, Microbial enzyme, Carbon dioxide." (The label panel in the Voilà images matches.)
- **Front name:** "LIBERTÉ COTTAGE CHEESE 2% M.F. 500 g" / "FROMAGE COTTAGE 2% M.G." (Voilà images).
- **Blue Cow:** A small blue rectangular logo with a cow silhouette shows on the side panel in the Voilà image, but it could not be read. There is no DFC cottage entry. **UNVERIFIED.**
- **Company's own words (retailer copy):** "[…] Combines the fresh taste of milk and the creaminess of cheese." The first sentence is a nutrition claim and is omitted.
- **Reading:** Front name is "cottage cheese"; **foreign-owned (Sodiaal, French co-operative)**; Blue Cow unverified.

### 2.12 Avalon (Avalon Dairy, Burnaby, BC)

- **Owner:** **UNVERIFIED.** The company says: "Avalon has been family-run since 1906" (https://avalondairy.com/). A 12 Jul 2016 China Daily report (Zhang Yu, Wang Wei) said Beijing Sanyuan Foods Co Ltd "plans to purchase a majority ownership in Crowley Properties Ltd, a Vancouver-based company making dairy products … for about C$19 million", via a joint venture in which Sanyuan would hold 51% (http://global.chinadaily.com.cn/a/201607/12/WS5a3105aea3108bc8c6731b4a.html). The article does not name Avalon. Avalon's own history names founder Jeremiah Crowley. **Completion of the deal and the current owner were not confirmed at tier (a) or (b). DO NOT state on air that Avalon is foreign-owned.** Pointers are in the UNVERIFIED list.
- **Where made:** The label and site give "7985 North Fraser Way, Burnaby, BC Canada V5J 0A4" (https://avalondairy.com/ ; label side panel on Save-On image https://images.cdn.saveonfoods.com/zoom/00066939102017.jpg). Whether cottage cheese is processed at that address is UNVERIFIED.
- **Line-up:** Organic Cottage Cheese 2% (500 g); Organic 1% (500 g) (Voilà 826565EA, 825821EA; Save-On).
- **Ingredients, 2% (verbatim, Save-On-Foods API `https://storefrontgateway.saveonfoods.com/api/stores/1982/products/00066939102017`):** "ORGANIC SKIM MILK, ORGANIC MILK, ORGANIC CREAM, ORGANIC SKIM MILK POWDER, BACTERIAL CULTURE, SALT, ORGANIC ARABIC GUM, ORGANIC GUAR GUM, ORGANIC LOCUST BEAN GUM, MICROBIAL ENZYME, CARBON DIOXIDE (TO MAINTAIN PRODUCT FRESHNESS." (The missing closing parenthesis is as published.)
- **Front name:** "AVALON Since 1906 / ORGANIC Cottage Cheese 2% M.F.", with the Canada Organic logo (Save-On image).
- **Blue Cow:** The lid shows a cow logo with "100% CANADIAN MILK". There is no DFC cottage entry, though Avalon is in DFC's brand list.
- **Company's own words:** "Meet the extended Avalon family. Organic butter, sour cream, yogurt, eggs, cheese, cottage cheese, kefir and of course, ice cream." (https://avalondairy.com/)
- **Reading:** Front name is "organic cottage cheese"; ownership UNVERIFIED; the lid shows a "100% Canadian Milk" cow logo.

### 2.13 Organic Meadow

- **Owner:** "Organic Meadow Limited Partnership" (footer, https://www.organicmeadow.com/). Partners UNVERIFIED.
- **Status:** **Discontinued.** CBC (7 May 2026, Natalie Stechyson) reports: "Canadian dairy producer Organic Meadow told CBC News that demand is so high that it had to discontinue its cottage cheese last September due to 'capacity constraints.'" The company is quoted: "We are actively exploring all options to address this situation and restore cottage cheese to our lineup." Today the site's product menu (butter, cheese, cream cheese, cream, grass-fed milk, ice cream, kefir, milk, sour cream, yogurt) has no cottage cheese, and `/products/organic-cottage-cheese/` returns 404.
- **Front name (historic):** "ORGANIC MEADOW / ORGANIC COTTAGE CHEESE 2%", from the DFC label image (https://dairyfarmersofcanada.ca/en/canadian-goodness/blue-cow-spotter/organic-meadow-organic-cottage-cheese-2-mf-500g).
- **Blue Cow:** DFC listing (it may be stale).
- **Ingredients:** Not available from a live brand or retailer page. UNVERIFIED.
- **Flag:** Healthy Planet's feed (via the Flipp ecom aggregator, tier c) still shows "Organic Meadow Cottage Cheese 2% 500g" at $8.99. **Do not use.**
- **Reading:** Canadian brand, cottage cheese discontinued Sept 2025 (company, via CBC).

## UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

Nothing in this list is confirmed at tier (a), (b) or (d). Pointers are given for follow-up.

1. **Avalon Dairy ownership.** "Family-run since 1906" (company) conflicts with a 2016 China Daily report of Beijing Sanyuan's planned 51% joint-venture purchase of "Crowley Properties Ltd". Completion and the link to Avalon are unconfirmed. Pointers: Beijing Sanyuan Foods (SSE 600429) 2016–2017 annual reports; BC corporate registry for Avalon Dairy Ltd. and Crowley Properties Ltd.; Pitchbook and Crunchbase (tier c, flag only) say "acquired 30-Sep-2016". **Do not say foreign-owned.**
2. **Plant locations** for Nordica (Clayson Road, Toronto is implied, not stated), Dairyland, Neilson, Baxter, Scotsburn, Beatrice, Liberté, Island Farms, Northumberland and Natrel Artisan-Style. Sealtest = Amqui rests on tier (b) reports only. Pointers: CFIA/SFCR licence search (https://inspection.canada.ca/en/food-licences), company plant pages, label plant codes.
3. **Meaning of the "441" hexagon** on the Nordica lid (possibly a plant registration number).
4. **Year Agropur acquired Island Farms** and **year Saputo acquired Dairyland**. Pointers: Agropur and Saputo annual reports (Saputo AIF); saputo.com history page.
5. **The Scotsburn trademark licensor.** The page says only "registered trademark … used under license by Saputo Dairy Products Canada G.P." Pointer: CIPO trademark database, SCOTSBURN.
6. **Year Beatrice joined Parmalat/Lactalis.** The Lactalis timeline does not name Beatrice. Pointer: lactalis.ca brand page for Beatrice; CIPO.
7. **Year General Mills acquired Liberté**, and the General Mills primary release on the Sodiaal sale (investors.generalmills.com returned 403; SEC 8-K returned 403).
8. **Where Liberté cottage cheese is made.** The side-panel text on the retailer image is illegible.
9. **Blue Cow status for Liberté** (a small blue logo is visible but illegible), **Natrel Artisan-Style**, and the **round blue badge on the Dairyland lid** (illegible).
10. **Island Farms front name.** DFC and its label photo say "Cottage Cheese Product"; the company site says "Cottage Cheese". Needs a current in-store label photo.
11. **Island Farms retail price.** No retailer page opened today lists it. Pointers: Save-On-Foods (other BC stores), Safeway/Thrifty Foods (Sobeys West), Walmart BC.
12. **Organic Meadow ingredients and owner partners.** The product is discontinued; the Healthy Planet listing ($8.99, via Flipp ecom feed) is tier (c) and possibly stale.
13. **Owners and makers of M-C Dairy, Bles-Wold, Pine River, Wolski Dairy and Mehadrin (500 g Québec)**: company sites were unreachable or blank. For **Mehadrin 453 g and J&J**, the country of manufacture is not shown in the images examined. Do not present as Canadian or U.S.
14. **Dairyland "#1 Most Trusted … 2026 BrandSpark"** (company website badge). This is a tier (d) candidate, but BrandSpark's methodology, sample and year were not opened. Pointer: https://www.BrandSparkMostTrusted.com
15. **Saputo Q1 FY2027 release** (https://newsroom.saputo.com/news-releases/news-release-details/saputo-reports-financial-results-first-quarter-fiscal-2027-ended) timed out. Its cottage capacity figure (30–40% at Friendship, N.Y.) is **U.S. context** and unverified.
16. **Prices and statistics quoted in news stories.** CBC and Charlebois: "roughly 60 per cent higher than in 2020"; CBC's quoted shelf prices at Metro, Sobeys, Loblaws and Walmart; StatCan production tonnages as quoted by CBC. **Not a retailer source; do not use as prices.** StatCan figures should be taken from StatCan directly; see the sibling file.
17. **Chatelaine article** "Why Is Canada Having A Cottage Cheese Shortage?" returned 403. Not used.
18. **Retailers that could not be read:** walmart.ca (bot check), metro.ca (403), foodbasics.ca (403), iga.net (403), sobeys.com (403), longos.com (403), Calgary Co-op shop (502), coopathome.ca (429), sameday.costco.ca search (JS only). **No Metro, Food Basics, IGA-branded, Walmart or Co-op shelf prices exist in this file**, apart from the flyer items listed.
19. **Voilà region.** Prices were taken at the default location with no postal code; the region is not displayed.
20. **Lucerne cottage cheese** in western Safeway stores: not checked (Sobeys West not reachable).
21. **Kawartha Dairy**: site returned 403; whether it sells cottage cheese is unknown.
22. **Riviera**: official site unreachable; no retailer listing found.
23. **Aggregator and SEO sources seen in search results and not used:** Wikipedia (Agropur, Saputo, Lactalis, Yoplait), Tracxn, Pitchbook, Crunchbase, Yahoo Finance reposts, investing.com transcripts, alicebusinessmag.com, stepbusinessadvice.com, thecoastguard.ca repost, AOL repost, madeinca.ca, X/Twitter posts, Reddit posts quoted inside CBC.
24. **Nordica Smooth "17% M.F."**: the DFC entry name is taken as a typo for 1.7%, which is not confirmed.

# Cottage cheese in Canada, 2026: private-label (store-brand) dossiers

Prepared 30 Sep 2026. Every source below was opened on 30 Sep 2026 unless a different date is given. Raw captures are saved under `scratchpad/priv/`. The file list is in Appendix B.

House rules applied throughout:
- No health, nutrition, protein, calorie, fat-as-health, sodium, culture-benefit, additive-safety or food-safety content. Front-panel nutrition claims and retailer marketing copy that contains nutrition language are omitted and marked "[claims omitted]".
- Recalls appear only in Appendix A.
- Ingredient lists are quoted verbatim. No ingredient is characterised.
- Tiers: (a) primary, (b) named outlet with byline and date, (c) aggregator (flag only), (d) named survey.
- Prices come only from a retailer's own site or API.
- Nothing here states or implies wrongdoing or motive.
- No U.S. formulations are presented as Canadian.
- Taste and texture are opinion.

Comparison vocabulary used in s. 3:
- **"match word for word"** means the same words in the same order. Differences in capital letters, a final full stop, or the separator (comma or bullet) are noted but are not treated as differences in wording.
- Anything else is described literally, e.g. "same order, except X".
- A match is never described as "made by the same plant" or "same maker". No source opened says that.

---

## 0. Headline findings (details and URLs in s. 2 and s. 3)

1. **Four private labels confirmed on a retailer's own site on 30 Sep 2026.** Each has a verbatim ingredient list:
   - No Name (Loblaw): 5 SKUs.
   - Compliments (Sobeys/Empire): 11 listings.
   - Selection and Life Smart / "Irrésistible Life Smart" (Metro): 4 listings.
   - Farm Boy (Empire): 1 pressed SKU.
2. **Kirkland Signature (Costco Canada) is carried.** Costco's own Business Centre site lists Kirkland Signature 2% Cottage Cheese 2 × 500 g and 3 × 750 g. Both show as out of stock and no price is displayed. Dairy Farmers of Canada lists both in its Blue Cow catalogue. No ingredient list was published on the pages opened.
3. **Not found on the retailer's own channel on 30 Sep 2026:**
   - President's Choice and PC Organics: 12 Loblaw-group stores searched, 9 banners, 5 provinces.
   - Compliments Organic.
   - Western Family and Our Finest: 6 Save-On-Foods stores.
   - Giant Tiger's house brand.
   - Co-op Gold.

   The following could not be read at all: Great Value (walmart.ca bot check), Longo's (403), and metro.ca's main host (403). Metro was read through its `api2.metro.ca` host.
4. **Strongest ingredient-list matches:**
   - **No Name 2% M.F. 750 g and No Name 1% M.F. 750 g (Loblaw)** and **Compliments 1% 500 g, listing 485854EA (Sobeys, Voilà page text)**: the two ingredient lists match word for word. The only difference is No Name's final full stop.
   - **Compliments 0.4% Cottage Cheese Dry Curd 600 g** and **Dairyland Dry Curd (Saputo)**: the two ingredient lists match word for word ("Skim milk, Bacterial culture, Microbial enzyme").
   - **Farm Boy Pressed Cottage Cheese 0.8% 500 g** and **M-C Dairy Cottage Cheese Pressed 0.8% M.F. 500 g**: the two ingredient lists match word for word ("Pasteurized Skim Milk", "Bacterial Culture"). Farm Boy separates the items with a bullet, M-C Dairy with a comma.
   - **Compliments 2% (the polysorbate 80 line)** and **Dairyland 2% / Baxter 2% (Saputo)**: the same 11 ingredients in the same order. One gum is named "Carob Bean Gum" on Compliments and "Locust Bean Gum" on the Saputo brands. The Saputo brands also add "(To Maintain Product Freshness)".
   - **No Name "Cottage Cheese" 500 g and No Name Club Size 1 kg** and **Beatrice 2% (Lactalis)**: the same 10 ingredients in the same order. The differences are "Carob Bean Gum" against "Locust bean gum" and Beatrice's "Contains: Milk".
   - **Metro Selection 1%** and **the Compliments 2% 500 g pack-panel image (440855EA)**: the same 10 ingredients in the same order. The only differences are one hyphen ("MONO AND" / "MONO- AND") and a final full stop.
5. **One Compliments listing contradicts itself.** For Compliments 2% 500 g (440855EA), the Voilà page text and the pack image on the same page give two different ingredient lists: different order, and "Carob Bean Gum" versus "LOCUST BEAN GUM". The pack image governs, but its date is unknown (UNVERIFIED, P9).
6. **Blue Cow logo:**
   - Seen on the Farm Boy pressed pack image (older "Dairy Farmers of Canada Quality Milk" version).
   - Seen on both Kirkland pack images; Kirkland is also listed in the DFC catalogue.
   - Not visible on any No Name or Compliments front or back image opened.
   - Kirkland Signature is the only private label in DFC's 32-item cottage-cheese catalogue.
7. **Maker or packer on record.** Only two items name one:
   - Farm Boy: the pack reads "PREPARED FOR / PRÉPARÉ POUR : FARM BOY COMPANY INC. OTTAWA ON …".
   - Kirkland: Costco's own product pages carry a data field, `"mfName"`, which reads "AGROPUR COOPERATIVE" for the 2 × 500 g item and "LACTALIS CANADA INC" for the 3 × 750 g item. This is a field in the page's embedded product record, not visible label text. What Costco uses the field for is not documented (P8).

   Every other co-packer is **unknown**.

Saved as `loblaw_q4_2025_nr.pdf`, from https://dis-prod.assetful.loblaw.ca/content/dam/loblaw-companies-limited/creative-assets/loblaw-ca/investor-relations-reports/annual/2025/LCL_Q4%202025_NR.pdf (opened 30 Sep 2026 in this project).

**Dated news (b).** CBC News, Natalie Stechyson, "No whey, a cottage cheese shortage? Demand surges amid protein craze", posted 7 May 2026 (updated 22 May), https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/cottage-cheese-shortage-canada-9.7187233 : "Loblaw also said its seeing increased demand for cottage cheese across its stores, and added, 'we are working closely with our suppliers to support this increased demand.'" This is a company statement about cottage cheese generally, not No Name specifically. The headline's word "protein" is the outlet's; do not repeat it on air.

**Context (b), not dairy-specific.** Canadian Grocer / The Canadian Press, Rosa Saba, 10/3/2024, https://canadiangrocer.com/private-label-food-got-more-popular-thanks-inflation-now-its-here-stay : "In its 2023 annual report, Loblaw noted that customers' increased focus on value 'benefited the company's sales due to its strength in private label products, discount banners and personalized promotions.'"

**Legal or regulatory items about Loblaw dairy private label, 2023–2026:** none found. The bread price-fixing settlement is not dairy and is not imported here.

## 3. Side-by-side: private-label ingredient lists against the national brands

National-brand lines are taken from the sibling file `scratchpad/cottage_brands_national.md` (retailer and company URLs in its s. 2) and from records re-opened today where noted. "Carob bean gum" and "locust bean gum" are compared **as printed**. No tier-(a) page opened today states that they are the same ingredient (P11).

### 3.1 Word-for-word matches

| Private label (source) | Matched line (source) | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| No Name 2% M.F. 750 g and 1% M.F. 750 g: "Milk Ingredients, Salt, Citric Acid, Guar Gum, Mono- And Diglycerides, Xanthan Gum, Carob Bean Gum, Bacterial Culture, Microbial Enzyme, Carbon Dioxide." (PC Express API, 20823988_EA / 20833357_EA, store 1080) | Compliments 1% 500 g: "Milk Ingredients, Salt, Citric Acid, Guar Gum, Mono- And Diglycerides, Xanthan Gum, Carob Bean Gum, Bacterial Culture, Microbial Enzyme, Carbon Dioxide" (https://voila.ca/products/485854EA/details). The Compliments 2% 500 g page text (440855EA) is the same apart from "&Nbsp;" encoding artefacts | **The two ingredient lists match word for word** (No Name ends with a full stop). Two different grocers' store brands. Their pack-panel images were not compared, because none is served for 485854EA and the 440855EA image differs from its own page text |
| Compliments 0.4% Dry Curd 600 g: "Skim milk, Bacterial culture, Microbial enzyme" (Voilà 484719EA, page text and pack image) | Dairyland Dry Curd 600 g: "Skim milk, Bacterial culture, Microbial enzyme." (dairyland.ca); "Skim Milk, Bacterial Culture, Microbial Enzyme." (PC Express 21672216_EA, RCSS 1517, re-opened today) | **The two ingredient lists match word for word** |
| Farm Boy Pressed 0.8% 500 g: "Pasteurized Skim Milk • Bacterial Culture." (farmboy.ca) | M-C Dairy "Cottage Cheese Pressed 0.8 % M.F." 500 g: "Pasteurized Skim Milk, Bacterial Culture." (PC Express 21720618_EA, store 1080, re-opened today) | **The two ingredient lists match word for word** (bullet against comma). Both are "0.8%" pressed, 500 g |

### 3.2 Same ingredients in the same order, with the named differences

| Private label | National brand | The only differences |
|---|---|---|
| Compliments 2% / 1% (500 g 484716EA, 484714EA; 750 g 429620EA, 485023EA, 429775EA, 484715EA): "Milk Ingredients, Salt, Carob Bean Gum, Guar Gum, Mono- And Diglycerides, Carrageenan, Xanthan Gum, Polysorbate 80, Bacterial Culture, Microbial Enzyme, Carbon Dioxide" | Dairyland 2% (Voilà 511640EA): "Milk Ingredients, Salt, Locust Bean Gum, Guar Gum, Mono- And Diglycerides, Carrageenan, Xanthan Gum, Polysorbate 80, Bacterial Culture, Microbial Enzyme, Carbon Dioxide (To Maintain Product Freshness)." ### 3.3 No close national match found

- **Kirkland Signature 2%:** the ingredient list is unavailable. The front declares "CONTAINS ULTRAFILTERED SKIM MILK AND CULTURED SKIM MILK". Among the national lines, "Ultrafiltered skim milk" and "Cultured skim milk" appear in Sealtest, Northumberland and Natrel Artisan-Style (Agropur) (sibling file s. 2.2–2.4). That is a shared ingredient word, not a list match; do not go further.
- **National lines with no private-label counterpart found:** Sealtest / Northumberland / Natrel (whole-milk-component lists), Island Farms, Liberté and Avalon.

# Cottage cheese: Canadian retail prices by brand, 29–30 Sep 2026, plus CPI and farm-gate context

Prepared 30 Sep 2026 (UTC). All retail prices below come from the retailers' own websites, apps or back-end APIs, or their own flyers. Each was fetched on 30 Sep 2026 between 01:42 and 02:05 UTC. For stores in Ontario, Quebec and the Atlantic provinces that is the evening of 29 Sep, local time. For stores in the West it is 29 Sep, afternoon or evening. Every listing carries the banner, the store or region the retailer showed, the pack size, the price and a computed unit price per 100 g. Nothing here comes from Flipp, PriceListo, Reddit, blogs or any price-tracker site.

**House rules applied:** this file contains no health, nutrition, protein, calorie, sodium, culture or food-safety content. Some retailer descriptions contain nutrition wording. It was **not** copied, except where a milk-fat percentage is needed to identify the product grade, and then only the percentage words are quoted. Brand-owner claims are attributed. No wrongdoing or motive is stated or implied. Price differences are reported as numbers only. Taste and texture are not discussed.

**Unit-price method:** $/100 g = shelf price ÷ net grams × 100, rounded to the cent. Multi-packs use total grams (4 × 113 g = 452 g; 2 × 500 g = 1,000 g). Listings sold by volume ("500 ml") cannot be converted to grams without a density figure. They are shown with the retailer's own $/100 ml and left out of the per-100 g rankings. The retailer's own unit price is shown alongside for checking. Where the two differ by a cent, that is rounding.

The Metro Ontario weekly flyer was opened: no cottage cheese is in it this week or next. **No prices** could be obtained from Metro online grocery, Walmart, IGA, Longo's, Co-op or Farm Boy's own site. Those are listed in UNVERIFIED.

## 2. Every listing, by banner

Column key: **Reg.** = the regular price shown. **Sale** = the promotional price shown, with the end date the retailer gave. **$/100 g** = computed from Reg. (or from Sale in the Sale column). **Retailer unit** = the unit price the retailer displayed. "Fat %" is taken from the product name unless noted.

On promotions: the API labels the three "Limit 5" items with price type `REGULAR` and reasonCode 4. It shows the lower price together with a deal badge reading "Limit 5, after limit $X". This file treats the lower figure as a limited promotion and the after-limit figure as the regular price. Both were re-confirmed on the product-detail endpoint at 02:03 UTC. The two `SPECIAL` items came with no regular or "was" price.
