What the table shows, as numbers only:
- **Voilà (default region):** two Compliments SKUs share the name "Compliments 2% Cottage Cheese 500 g" at different prices: 484716EA at $5.15 and 440855EA at $5.59. The same holds at 750 g: 485023EA at $6.49 and 429620EA at $7.79, and for 1% (484714EA $5.15 / 485854EA $5.59; 484715EA $6.49 / 429775EA $7.79). The site does not say why. The comparisons above use the lower-priced SKU. At this banner, Neilson 2% 500 g ($4.99) is listed **below** both Compliments 2% 500 g SKUs.
- **Loblaw banners:** in every same-store pair in this table, No Name was priced at or below the national brand it was compared with. At Provigo 7297 they were equal: No Name 500 g and Sealtest 2% 500 g were both $5.49. The Halifax pairs use a No Name SPECIAL price, since no regular price was returned, so they are not a regular-price comparison.
- **Save-On-Foods and Giant Tiger:** no private-label cottage cheese appeared in either search. The Save-On "Cottage Cheese" category held 10 items, all national or regional brands.

## 6. Anomalies and data notes (do not quote without re-checking)

- **Mehadrin "Cottage Cheese 0.01" 12x453.0 g, $13.99, RCSS Vancouver 1517 (code 21387030_EA).** The API gives pack size "12x453.0 g" and a unit price of $0.26/100 g. Other single Mehadrin tubs at the same store are $11.99–$15.99. The pack size looks like a data-entry error. It was confirmed on the detail endpoint at 02:03:34 UTC, still "12x453.0 g" and $13.99. It is left out of all rankings.
- Loblaw listings with size "500 ml" (Dairyland 2% at RCSS and No Frills; Scotsburn Low Fat at Atlantic Superstore) are volume-labelled in the retailer data. Their retailer unit price is per 100 ml, so they are left out of the per-100 g rankings.
- Save-On-Foods returned identical prices for Langley (1982) and Calgary Heritage (6634). Winnipeg St James (4415) differed on the two 750 g Dairyland tubs ($8.15 vs $8.25).
- Voilà shows no province. The session did not enter a postal code, and the page state gives `regionName: "Default Region 1"`.
- Giant Tiger shows one online price per product with no store selector. The province codes come from the product tags (`mms_province_code`), and every cottage cheese item is tagged `in_store_only:true`. The price in a given store may differ; that is not verified.
- Costco.ca: the item was listed online as "OutOfStock". Costco.ca online prices may differ from warehouse prices; that is not verified.
- Excluded as not cottage cheese by name: "MC Dairy Value Pack Polski Tworg 750 g" ($12.79, Voilà); "No Name Marble Farmer's Cheese 400 g"; and prepared foods whose names include "cottage cheese" (Cheemo perogies; Western Family perogies; Kitchens of India curries).

## 7. Statistics Canada CPI, table 18-10-0004-01 (monthly, not seasonally adjusted, Canada)

Source: the Statistics Canada CSV download for table 18-10-0004-01, fetched 30 Sep 2026 about 02:02 UTC:
- https://www150.statcan.gc.ca/t1/tbl1/en/dtl!downloadDbLoadingData-nonTraduit.action?pid=1810000401&latestN=50&startDate=&endDate=&csvLocale=en&selectedMembers=%5B%5B2%5D%2C%5B22%5D%5D&checkedLevels= (Dairy products, member 22)
- same URL with member 25 (Cheese) and member 326 ("Unripened or fresh cheese (2013=100)"), and with members 2,4 (All-items; Food purchased from stores), latestN=25

**How it was retrieved:** direct curl to www150.statcan.gc.ca failed with connection resets at 01:58, 01:59 and 02:00 UTC. The proxy logged `ws_closed_mid_exchange`. The same Statistics Canada CSV URLs were then opened with the WebFetch tool, which returns the file as text. The values below are that text. As a cross-check, the same method was used on table 18-10-0245-01 (§8). Its values matched, to the cent, a CSV of that table downloaded from Statistics Canada on 29 Sep 2026 (Block cheese Jul 2024 $6.84; Jun 2026 $7.03; Jul 2026 $7.11; Yogurt Jul 2024 $3.56; Jul 2026 $3.84). The CPI values themselves should still be re-checked against the table before broadcast (see UNVERIFIED).

"Dairy products" and "Cheese" are on the table's standard base. The product name of the "Unripened or fresh cheese" series itself states "(2013=100)". Index levels on different bases can be compared only as percentage changes, not as levels. Cottage cheese has no series of its own in this table. "Unripened or fresh cheese" is the narrowest category that would include it; the category's composition was not verified today.

## 8. Statistics Canada average retail prices, table 18-10-0245-01: is cottage cheese a series?

**No. Table 18-10-0245-01 ("Monthly average retail prices for selected products") has no cottage cheese series.**

- Table page https://www150.statcan.gc.ca/t1/tbl1/en/tv.action?pid=1810024501 was opened via WebFetch at about 02:02 UTC. It gives the title "Monthly average retail prices for selected products", monthly, with the data grid not rendered in static HTML.
- The product list was read from the metadata file inside the table's full CSV download from Statistics Canada (`18100245.zip`, downloaded 29 Sep 2026; metadata lists end reference period 2026-07-01). It holds 111 product members. The only cheese series is "Block cheese, 500 grams" (member 18). The other dairy series are "Milk, 1 litre", "Milk, 2 litres", "Milk, 4 litres", "Cream, 1 litre", "Butter, 454 grams" and "Yogurt, 500 grams". No member name contains "cottage". Because the metadata file was downloaded on 29 Sep, not 30 Sep, the "no cottage series" finding rests on a download one day old plus today's check of the table page.
- For context, the CSV download (WebFetch, about 02:02 UTC, https://www150.statcan.gc.ca/t1/tbl1/en/dtl!downloadDbLoadingData-nonTraduit.action?pid=1810024501&latestN=25&startDate=&endDate=&csvLocale=en&selectedMembers=%5B%5B11%5D%2C%5B18%2C19%5D%5D&checkedLevels=) gives Canada averages of: Block cheese, 500 grams: $6.84 (Jul 2024), $6.94 (Jul 2025), $7.11 (Jul 2026). Yogurt, 500 grams: $3.56 (Jul 2024), $3.70 (Jul 2025), $3.84 (Jul 2026). July 2026 is the latest month in the table.

## 9. Canadian Dairy Commission: farm-gate milk price adjustments announced 2022–2026

Index page: https://www.cdc-ccl.ca/en/pricing-announcements (opened 02:00:02 UTC). Each announcement below was opened at the time shown. Quotes are verbatim.

| Announced | Effective | Adjustment | Verbatim CDC line | URL (opened, UTC) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Nov 2022 | 1 Feb 2023 | +2.2% | "The farmgate milk price will increase by 2.2% which translates to $0.0174/litre (less than 2 cents per litre)." | https://www.cdc-ccl.ca/en/node/943 (02:00:31) |
| 1 Nov 2023 | postponed from 1 Feb 2024 to **1 May 2024** | +1.77% | "the CDC has postponed the farmgate milk price adjustment, representing an increase of 1.77%, to May 1, 2024, instead of February 1, 2024.  This increase translates to $0.0153/litre (or just over 1 cent per litre)." | https://www.cdc-ccl.ca/en/adjustment-farmgate-price-milk-postponed-may-1-2024 (02:00:29) |
| 1 Nov 2024 | 1 Feb 2025 | −0.0237% | "The farmgate price of milk will decrease by 0.0237%, which translates to less than 1 cent per litre of milk sold to processors." | https://www.cdc-ccl.ca/en/farmgate-milk-price-decrease-2025 (02:00:26) |
| 31 Oct 2025 | 1 Feb 2026 | +2.3255% (NPF result); +2.3750% combined with carrying charges | "The result of the National Pricing Formula (NPF) … is an increase of 2.3255%." and "The combined effect of the NPF and the adjustment to carrying charges will result in an increase in the cost of milk used to make dairy products such as milk, cream, yogurt, cheese and butter for the retail and restaurant sectors of 2.3750%, which translates to just over 2 cents per litre of milk sold to processors to produce finished dairy products." | https://www.cdc-ccl.ca/en/2026-increase-farmgate-milk-price-aligned-inflation (02:00:24) |

Related verbatim context:
- 2023 process. The CDC page of 13 Oct 2023 says: "The CDC received a request from the Canadian Federation of Independent Grocers (CFIG). As a result, the routine pricing process will be suspended pending consultations with stakeholders." https://www.cdc-ccl.ca/en/national-pricing-formula-and-february-1-2024-farmgate-price-milk-adjustments (02:00:29). This records a request and a process step. It is not a finding about anyone.
- May 2024 implementation. The CDC update says: "New milk component prices were subsequently approved by all ten provincial authorities on December 11, 2023." and "All price adjustments will come into effect on May 1, 2024." https://www.cdc-ccl.ca/en/update-may-1st-2024-price-changes (02:00:28)
- Pass-through. In the 2026 announcement the CDC says: "The net impact of these increases on the final cost of dairy products is unknown since prices are also influenced by incremental factors further along the supply chain such as labour, transportation, distribution and packaging costs. A change in price paid to farmers for their milk does not necessarily translate to a similar consumer price change."
- Regulation scope. The same page says: "only the price of milk that farmers get is regulated. With the exception of fluid milk in some provinces, the retail price of dairy products is not regulated in Canada."
- The CDC's own CPI comparisons (the CDC's claims, not re-derived here) are as follows. 2025 announcement: "Over the past year, the average annual Consumer Price Index (CPI) for dairy products increased by 2.7%, a rate similar to the overall food category, which increase by 2.5%." 2024 announcement: "In the last year, the average annual consumer price index for dairy products increased at a slightly lower rate (2.0%) than all food products (3.4%)."
- **No announcement yet for 2027.** At 02:00:50 UTC on 30 Sep 2026, "Latest News for 2026" (https://www.cdc-ccl.ca/en/latest-news-2026) listed only "Meeting notice - Annual public meeting of the Canadian Dairy Commission (January 15th, 2026)". The pricing-announcements index lists nothing dated 2026. The CDC says decisions are "usually announced by mid-December and becomes effective on February 1st of the following year" (index page). The recent announcements are dated 31 Oct to 1 Nov.
- Also on the index page, not farm-gate adjustments for all of Canada: Newfoundland and Labrador fluid-milk items (approved adjustment 1 Sep 2025; "will not move forward with a pricing adjustment on February 1st, 2025", 3 Dec 2024).

## Appendix A: recalls (DO NOT USE ON AIR)

No recall research was done for this price file, and no recall is listed here. Under the house rules, any recall found elsewhere belongs only in an appendix marked "do not use on air".

## Appendix B: method and scripts (scratchpad/cp/)

- `lpull.py`: Loblaw search API pull. `sof.py`: Save-On-Foods preview queries. `gt.py` and `gt2.py`: Giant Tiger suggest and product JSON. `build.py`: normalises all listings into `listings.json`. `md.py`: writes this file. `cpi.py`: the CPI values as returned.
- Raw responses are saved in the same folder: `voila_search.html`, `voila_cat.html`, `voila_state.json`, `voila_cat_state.json`, `lraw/*.json`, `pl_*.json`, `det_*.json`, `sof_{1982,6634,4415}.json`, `sof_stores_fresh.json`, `gt_cottage.json`, `costco_p.html`, `metro_84224.pdf/.txt`, `metro_84225.pdf/.txt`, `mfl.json`, and `cdc/*.html`.

## UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE

Items that could not be confirmed at tier (a), (b) or (d), or that need a re-check before use:

1. **Metro online grocery prices** (metro.ca, foodbasics.ca, superc.ca): 403 captcha on every search and aisle URL (§1). No Metro, Food Basics or Super C cottage cheese price is available. The Metro Ontario flyer (store #136 Devonshire Mall, 24–30 Sep and 1–7 Oct) was opened and has no cottage cheese in its text layer. A flyer item carried only as an image would not appear in a text search; that is not verified.
2. **Walmart Canada prices**: bot-block page ("Verify Your Identity") on search, browse and product URLs. Walmart's flyer is served by Flipp and was not used (no-Flipp rule).
3. **IGA (iga.net)**: 403 on all URLs.
4. **Longo's**: 403.
5. **Co-op (Calgary Co-op, Federated/CRS, coop.ca)**: the shop host was refused by the egress proxy (502), and other hosts gave no prices.
6. **Farm Boy's own site** shows no price. The only Farm Boy price is the Voilà listing (Farm Boy 0.8% Cottage Cheese Pressed 500 g, $8.49).
7. **loblaws.ca, nofrills.ca, realcanadiansuperstore.ca** web pages: 403. pcexpress.ca: JavaScript shell only. Loblaw-banner prices come from the Loblaw PC Express API that those sites use (tier (a) retailer back end). Before broadcast, spot-check them in the app or on a store shelf.
8. **Voilà region**: no province is shown ("Default Region 1"). Do not attribute Voilà prices to a particular province. Five of the 55 search results were not rendered and are not captured.
9. **Giant Tiger** prices are online list prices tagged "in-store only". Store-level prices were not verified.
10. **Costco.ca** M-C Dairy price: online listing, marked out of stock. The warehouse price was not verified.
11. **Mehadrin 12x453.0 g at $13.99** (RCSS Vancouver): the pack size looks like a data error (§6). Do not use.
12. **Brand ownership of Foremost and Beatrice**: not verified today. Do not describe either as a private label or name an owner without a company source.
13. **Mehadrin, J and J**: where these brands and products are made was not checked. Do not describe them as Canadian or U.S. products.
14. **CPI values (§7)**: taken from Statistics Canada CSV URLs via WebFetch, because direct curl to www150.statcan.gc.ca was reset by the network path. The method matched a 29 Sep direct download for table 18-10-0245-01. Still, re-check the Aug 2024, Aug 2025 and Aug 2026 values in the table viewer before quoting.
15. **Table 18-10-0245-01 "no cottage cheese series"**: based on the table's metadata file downloaded 29 Sep 2026 plus the 30 Sep page check. Re-confirm on the day of use.
16. **Loblaw "Limit 5" prices** ($2.43 Nordica 2% 500 g at RCSS Calgary; $4.73 and $5.63 Dairyland at RCSS Vancouver): the API types them as REGULAR, with a limit badge. Confirm how they appear to shoppers before calling them a "sale" on air.
17. The Loblaw and Save-On product descriptions contain nutrition and health wording. **None of it may be used** (house rule 1). Only the milk-fat percentage wording has been reproduced, to identify a grade.
18. Anything in the February 2026 cottage cheese video about protein or nutrition is **not** supported by this file and must not be reused.


<!-- ===== cottage_industry_record.md ===== -->

# The Canadian cottage cheese industry: the documented record, 2023 to 2026

Prepared 30 Sep 2026. Every fact below carries the URL that was opened and the date it was opened. Unless a row says otherwise, the date is 30 Sep 2026. Primary sources (tier a) come first in every section. Anything that could not be confirmed at tier (a), (b) or (d) is in the final section, "UNVERIFIED / DO-NOT-USE".

**House rules applied (hard)**
- There are no health, nutrition, protein, calorie, fat, sodium, culture, additive, gut or food-safety claims in this file. Some source headlines and quotes use protein or health framing. Headlines appear only as citations and are marked **[H]**, meaning "headline wording is not for on-air use". Quotes were trimmed so that no health or protein sentence is reproduced, and each trim is noted.
- Recalls and food-safety items appear only in Appendix A, "do not use on air".
- There are no retail prices in this file. Rule 5 allows prices only from a retailer's own site. Prices quoted by news outlets are listed under UNVERIFIED as outlet-reported.
- Legal and regulatory items carry a label: allegation, finding, settlement, admission, order, or "U.S. executive finding (U.S. claim)".
- U.S. material is labelled **U.S. CONTEXT** or **U.S. CLAIM**.
- Taste and texture: none are asserted.

**Tier key:** (a) primary · (b) named outlet with byline and date · (c) aggregator, flag only · (d) named survey with methodology.

**How pages were opened:** curl through the agent proxy works for cbc.ca, radio-canada.ca, ctvnews.ca, globalnews.ca, whitehouse.gov, agropur.com, gaylea.com, lactalis.ca, angusreid.org and leger360.com. It fails with "connection reset" for all *.gc.ca hosts: statcan, agriculture.canada.ca, cbsa-asfc, international.gc.ca and canada.ca. Those pages were opened with WebFetch, and the method is marked **WF**. Where WebFetch paraphrased, the text is labelled "as returned by WF", and it should be re-read in a browser before any verbatim use on air.

---

## 1. Production of cottage cheese in Canada (tier a)

### 1a. Annual production, Canada, tonnes (Statistics Canada)

The 2010–2024 figures are the sum of monthly values in Table 32-10-0112-01 (A2). The 2025 figure is the sum of four quarters in Table 32-10-0483-01 (A1). The table note reads: **"Data include both creamed and uncreamed cottage cheese."** The monthly table also notes: **"Starting January 2012, volumes of fruit and/or additives are excluded."** A third note: **"This publication transitioned from a monthly to a quarterly frequency in February 2025. Monthly values from 1970 to 2024 are available in table 32-10-0112."** The survey is the Dairy Factory Production and Stocks Survey (3430).

| Year | Tonnes (StatCan) | AAFC Cheese Sector Profile (A4) | Change vs prior year (StatCan) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2010 | 26,019 | – | – |
| 2014 | 24,094 | 24,094 | – |
| 2015 | 21,362 | – | – |
| 2016 | 21,397 | – | +0.2% |
| 2017 | 23,339 | – | +9.1% |
| 2018 | 23,528 | – | +0.8% |
| 2019 | 23,255 | – | −1.2% |
| 2020 | 24,101 | 24,101 | +3.6% |
| 2021 | 23,181 | 23,079 | −3.8% |
| 2022 | 22,066 | 22,066 | −4.8% |
| 2023 | 23,474 | 23,474 | +6.4% |
| 2024 | 28,139 | 28,139 | **+19.9%** |
| 2025 | **35,459** (sum of quarters) | not yet published | **+26.0%** |

- 2025 is 66% above 2015 (35,459 ÷ 21,362).
- The 2021 figures differ: the StatCan CSV sums to 23,181 and the AAFC PDF prints 23,079. This is probably a revision after the AAFC calculation. Use the StatCan figure and say it is the current StatCan figure.
- A WDS query today also returned a WebFetch-summarised set of annual sums that do not match the CSV. For example, it gave 28,305 for 2024 against the exact monthly values of 2,334 + 2,252 + 2,178 + 2,314 + 2,226 + 2,179 + 2,487 + 2,524 + 2,283 + 2,691 + 2,497 + 2,174 = 28,139. Those WebFetch sums were discarded. The monthly values themselves were confirmed live (A2).
- AAFC's print (A4): the profile gives total cheese production of **528,207 metric tonnes** in 2024. Cottage cheese is therefore about 5.3% of the total (28,139 ÷ 528,207). That percentage is computed here, not published.

### 1b. Monthly, 2023–2024 (tonnes, Canada; A2)

| Month | 2023 | 2024 |
|---|---|---|
| Jan | 1,879 | 2,334 |
| Feb | 1,770 | 2,252 |
| Mar | 1,924 | 2,178 |
| Apr | 2,128 | 2,314 |
| May | 1,896 | 2,226 |
| Jun | 2,056 | 2,179 |
| Jul | 1,935 | 2,487 |
| Aug | 1,928 | 2,524 |
| Sep | 1,961 | 2,283 |
| Oct | 2,175 | 2,691 |
| Nov | 1,998 | 2,497 |
| Dec | 1,824 | 2,174 |

December 2024 is 19.2% above December 2023. CBC (B1) rounded this to "20 per cent".

### 1c. Quarterly, 2025–2026 (tonnes, Canada; A1, confirmed live via WDS)

| Quarter | 2025 | 2026 | y/y |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 (Jan–Mar) | 8,571 | 9,870 | +15.2% |
| Q2 (Apr–Jun) | 8,923 | 10,546 | +18.2% |
| Q3 (Jul–Sep) | 8,445 | not yet released | – |
| Q4 (Oct–Dec) | 9,520 | – | – |
| H1 total | 17,494 | 20,416 | +16.7% |

- The provincial rows for Quebec, Ontario, Manitoba, Alberta and British Columbia are all "x", which StatCan defines as "suppressed to meet the confidentiality requirements of the Statistics Act". No provincial split can be published.
- Q2 2026 (10,546 t) is the highest of the six quarterly values in Table 32-10-0483. For comparison, the highest three-month sum in the old monthly table for 2024 is Aug–Oct 2024: 2,524 + 2,283 + 2,691 = 7,498 t. That sum is computed here. Q1 2025 (8,571 t) is 26.7% above Q1 2024 as summed from the monthly table (2,334 + 2,252 + 2,178 = 6,764 t).
- **Caution:** 2025 is the first year of the quarterly survey. StatCan's note describes a frequency change only; it does not flag a break in the series. The jump from 2024 to 2025 should still be described as "according to Statistics Canada's quarterly figures". Ask StatCan (Agriculture Division) whether coverage changed before calling it a record.

### 1d. Other CDIC context (A5)

- CDIC "Dairy industry at a glance" (as returned by WF): "Number of plants: 519 dairy plants" (2025), and "Cheese (479.5 thousand tonnes)". The WF extract did not show the year for the 479.5 thousand tonnes figure, and it does not match A4's 528,207 t for 2024, which includes processed cheese categories. **Do not use the 479.5 figure until its year is confirmed.**
- dairyinfo.gc.ca now serves only a bilingual gateway (A5). The old CDIC "production by variety" table URL returned 404: https://agriculture.canada.ca/en/sector/animal-industry/canadian-dairy-information-centre/statistics-market-information/dairy-products

## 2. Per-capita figures (tier a)

StatCan does not publish a "consumption" series. It publishes **"Food available"**, a disappearance measure, in Table 32-10-0054-01 (A3). Present it as "cottage cheese available per person", not "eaten per person".

| Year | Food available, kg per person per year (v108730) | Adjusted for losses (v41356479) |
|---|---|---|
| 2014 | 0.68 | 0.41 |
| 2015 | 0.60 | 0.36 |
| 2016 | 0.59 | 0.36 |
| 2017 | 0.64 | 0.39 |
| 2018 | 0.63 | 0.39 |
| 2019 | 0.62 | 0.38 |
| 2020 | 0.63 | 0.38 |
| 2021 | 0.61 | 0.37 |
| 2022 | 0.57 | 0.34 |
| 2023 | 0.59 | 0.36 |
| 2024 | 0.68 | 0.41 |
| **2025** | **0.85** | **0.52** |

- The live WDS release time for all points is 2026-05-28 08:30.
- 2025 is 25% above 2024 (0.85 ÷ 0.68). It is the highest value since at least 2010 (2010: 0.77; 2011: 0.75; 2012: 0.75).
- AAFC (A4) publishes per-capita cheese only at the "Total All Cheeses" level: "15.24 kg in 2024". It does not publish a cottage cheese line. The profile's words: "Total annual cheese consumption increased from 13.59 kg per capita in 2014 to 15.24 kg in 2024."

## 3. Imports of fresh cheese (HS 0406.10) by country (tier a)

**Classification caveat (important).** Cottage cheese has had **no separate import code since 1991**. HS10 0406.10.00.20 "Cottage cheese" was valid only from 1988-01 to 1991-12 (A6, ODPF_1_HS10Desc.TXT). Cottage cheese now falls in **0406.10.10.90, "Cheese, fresh, nes, including whey cheese, unfermented and curd, w/a commitment"**. That line also covers every other fresh cheese except cream cheese (for example ricotta, mascarpone, quark and fresh curd). **Import figures therefore cannot be presented as cottage cheese imports.** Say "fresh cheese, including cottage cheese".

HS10 lines inside 0406.10:
- 0406.10.10.10 "Cream cheese, o/t whey and buttermilk cheese, within access commitment"
- 0406.10.10.90 "Cheese, fresh, nes, including whey cheese, unfermented and curd, w/a commitment"
- 0406.10.20.00 "Cheese, fresh, nes, including whey cheese, unfermented and curd, o/a commitment" (over access)

### 3a. Totals (A6; kg and CAD value)

| HS10 | 2024 kg | 2024 $ | 2025 kg | 2025 $ | Jan–Jul 2025 kg | Jan–Jul 2026 kg |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0406.10.10.10 cream cheese, within access | 2,556,019 | 15,384,914 | 2,951,320 | 18,560,273 | 1,691,501 | 1,457,556 |
| 0406.10.10.90 fresh cheese nes incl. curd, within access | 4,000,879 | 33,137,239 | 5,652,400 | 49,883,504 | 2,956,054 | 3,071,634 |
| 0406.10.20.00 over access | 14,302 | 77,446 | 9,948 | 66,629 | 4,864 | 3,330 |
| **All 0406.10** | **6,571,200** | – | **8,613,668** | – | – | – |

- Cross-check: AAFC's "Fresh" natural cheese imports for 2024 are **6,571 t** (A4). That matches the CIMT total for all of 0406.10, cream cheese included, to the tonne. AAFC's "Fresh" line therefore includes cream cheese.
- AAFC "Fresh" imports, t (A4): 2014 519 · 2020 1,967 · 2021 2,585 · 2022 3,105 · 2023 4,688 · 2024 6,571.
- Fresh cheese nes (0406.10.10.90) imports rose 41.3% in 2025 (5,652 t vs 4,001 t). The 2025 import volume in this line is about 16% of domestic cottage cheese production (5,652 ÷ 35,459). That comparison is computed here, and the import line is not all cottage cheese.
- Average declared value, 0406.10.10.90: $8.28/kg in 2024 and $8.83/kg in 2025. These are computed customs values, **not retail prices**.
- Declared destination province, 0406.10.10.90: 2025 ON 3,102,705 kg · QC 2,027,005 · BC 477,681 · MB 18,452 · SK 17,962 · AB 8,595.

## 4. Tariffs and the cheese tariff-rate-quota structure (tier a)

### 4a. Over-quota tariff for fresh cheese (CBSA T2026, A7; as returned by WF)

| Tariff item | Description (verbatim) | MFN tariff (verbatim) |
|---|---|---|
| 0406.10.10 | "Fresh (unripened or uncured) cheese, including whey cheese, and curd - Within access commitment" | "3.32¢/kg" (UST, CEUT, CPTPT, UKT: "Free") |
| 0406.10.20 | "Fresh (unripened or uncured) cheese, including whey cheese, and curd - Over access commitment" | **"245.5% but not less than $4.52/kg"** |

- WF returned no preferential rate for 0406.10.20. Before quoting on air, confirm in a browser that the over-access rate applies to U.S. (UST) and EU (CEUT) goods alike.
- CBC (B12, Mar 14, 2025) independently reported: "in 2025, Canada would apply a 298.5 per cent levy on above-maximum butter and 245.5 per cent for above-maximum cheddar cheese. Those were the same rates applied in 2017 and 2020."

### 4b. Cheese TRQ access quantities, 2026 calendar year (GAC key dates page, A8, modified 2026-09-29)

| Agreement / TRQ | Access quantity (kg) | "Available Quantity" shown* |
|---|---|---|
| WTO Cheeses of All Types | 20,411,866 | – |
| CETA Cheese of All Types | 16,000,000 | – |
| CETA Industrial Cheese | 1,700,000 | 589,760 |
| CPTPP Cheeses of All Types | 3,735,000 | – |
| CPTPP Industrial Cheese | 8,217,000 | 6,436,087 |
| CPTPP Mozzarella and Prepared Cheese | 2,988,000 | 298,931 |
| CUSMA Cheeses of All Types | 6,313,000 | – |
| CUSMA Industrial Cheeses | 6,313,000 | 2,468,453 |

\* The footnote as returned by WF reads: "* Available Quantity for the 2026 Calendar Year". The page does not say (in the WF extract) whether this means unallocated or returned quantity. Do not interpret it on air.

### 4c. Who may hold cheese quota: the allocation rules (GAC notices; as returned by WF)

| Notice | Retailers eligible? | Allocation basis (quoted as returned) |
|---|---|---|
| CUSMA Cheeses of All Types, Serial No. 1079, May 16, 2022 (A9) | **No.** "Retailers are **not** eligible for an allocation. A retailer is an establishment that is primarily engaged in retailing food, and which buys cheeses of all types and sells it directly to final consumers." | Processors: "based on the kilograms of cheeses of all types manufactured…during the reference period"; distributors: "based on the kilograms of cheese sold…during the reference period" |
| CETA Cheese of All Types, Serial No. 993, Oct 1, 2020 (A10) | **Yes, in the distributor/retailer pools.** "20% is allocated to large distributors and retailers whose initial calculated allocation…equals or exceeds 20,000 kilograms"; "30% is allocated to small and medium-sized distributors and retailers…" | "20% is allocated to large cheese manufacturers that use more than 50,000,000 liters of milk annually"; "30% is allocated to small and medium-sized cheese manufacturers…"; new entrants "at least 10%" from 2022 |
| CPTPP Cheeses of All Types, Serial No. 1143, Oct 1, 2025 (A11) | **No.** Retailers "are **not** eligible to apply for an allocation." | Processors by kg manufactured; distributors by kg sold |
| WTO Cheeses of All Types, Serial No. 998, Oct 1, 2020 (A12) | (not extracted) | "69.9% is allocated to cheese imports from EU Member States; 30.1% is allocated to cheese imports from non-EU sources" |

This retailer-eligibility difference between the CUSMA and CETA notices is the exact ground of the U.S. July 2026 proclamation (section 8).

### 4d. Canadian law on future trade concessions (A14, A15)

- Bill C-202 (45-1), "An Act to amend the Department of Foreign Affairs, Trade and Development Act (supply management)". Sponsor: Yves-François Blanchet. First reading May 29, 2025. Passed the House June 5, 2025. Senate: WF returned "June 17, 2025". **Royal Assent June 26, 2025.** S.C. 2025, c. 1 (A15). The Senate date looks inconsistent with the rest and should be rechecked.
- Resulting s. 10(2.1) (A14; as returned by WF): the Minister must not make any commitment, by international trade treaty or agreement, that would have the effect of "(a) increasing the tariff rate quota, within the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the Customs Tariff, applicable to dairy products, poultry or eggs" or "(b) reducing the tariff applicable to those goods when they are imported in excess of the applicable tariff rate quota".

### 4e. CUSMA dispute panels on Canada's dairy TRQs (tribunal record, A13)

| Case | Dates (GAC, verbatim as returned) | Label |
|---|---|---|
| U.S. v. Canada, dairy TRQ allocation (first) | "2020-12-09: The United States requests consultations." "2022-01-04: Panel releases its final report." "On May 16, 2022, Canada published new CUSMA dairy TRQ allocation and administration policies" | **Panel finding** (the GAC page says Canada modified its policies in response; the report's findings were not opened here) |
| U.S. v. Canada, dairy TRQ allocation (second) | "2023-01-31: The United States requests the establishment of a panel." "2023-11-24: The final report was made public." | **Panel finding:** "The panel found in Canada's favour on all claims made by the United States" |

## 5. The processors: Canadian footprint and dated events 2023–2026 (company sources first)

### 5a. Agropur Cooperative (Sealtest, Natrel, Island Farms, Farmers, Québon, Lucerne and others)

**Company facts (A17, A19)**
- "Agropur is the largest dairy cooperative in Canada… Founded in 1938… 2,700 members based in Quebec, New Brunswick, and Nova Scotia, as well as for its 7,000 employees." Revenues: "$8.9 billion CAD in 2025", and "processed 6.7 billion liters of milk" (A17, July 15, 2026). The company says so.
- Plant count in the boilerplate: "Operating 29 plants in Canada and the United States" (Feb 11 and April 23, 2026 releases, A19 and A18). It became "Operating 28 plants" in the July 15, 2026 release (A17).
- AAFC (A4) describes Agropur as "one of the largest dairy cooperatives in North America". The profile's figures ("over 3,000 dairy farmers members and 8,800 employees") are older than the company's 2026 figures.

**Where Agropur makes cottage cheese**
- Radio-Canada (B8, Dec 10, 2025): "Seulement deux usines au Canada produisent du fromage cottage, soit celle d'Amqui et celle de Victoria, en Colombie-Britannique." This is an outlet statement. Read in context, it means only two Agropur plants in Canada make cottage cheese: Amqui and Victoria, B.C.
- Agropur's spokesperson, quoted by Radio-Canada (B7, Nov 21, 2025): "Il faut comprendre qu'Amqui est l'une de nos deux usines Agropur à produire du fromage cottage et de la crème sure."
- Radio-Canada (B6/B7): "L'usine Natrel d'Amqui produit plus de 30 millions de litres de lait annuellement, étant spécialisée dans le fromage cottage et la crème sure."
- Don Mills (Toronto) plant (A20, Aug 12, 2026): the company calls it "Agropur's largest fluid milk plant in Canada". Its product list is "regular milk, lactose-free milk, cream and mixes used for soft-serve ice cream". It does **not** list cottage cheese.

**Amqui labour dispute and cottage cheese supply (tier b; Radio-Canada)**

| Date | Event | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 12–13 Oct 2025 | The union began a 24-hour strike at 19 h Sunday. On Monday, 13 Oct, management declared a lock-out of about 100 employees. Agropur's Guillaume Bérubé, as quoted in paraphrase: "l'approvisionnement en fromage cottage et en crème sure sera réduit puisque l'usine d'Amqui est la principale à fabriquer ces produits de culture au pays. Les épiceries se retrouveront donc en pénurie de ces deux produits d'ici quelques jours, selon M. Bérubé." | B5 (Oct 13, 2025) |
| 28–29 Oct 2025 | Agropur confirmed that all cheese production (cheddar and curd) at Amqui would move elsewhere, with job losses. The union put the cheese line at "de 10 à 15 personnes". Bérubé: "Il n'y a pas de bon moment pour faire de telles annonces, mais c'était important pour nous de faire preuve de transparence avec le syndicat." The outlet reports: "Face à la popularité grandissante des produits protéinés, Agropur souhaite investir et augmenter sa production de fromage cottage." **[H]**-type framing; paraphrase only. Members rejected the employer's second offer "à 95 %" on Monday (27 Oct). | B6 (Oct 29, 2025) |
| 21 Nov 2025 | The lock-out ended. Members accepted the offer "à 70 %". Average annual raise "3,31 %". Start of the lock-out: "Le lock-out a débuté le 14 octobre" (B7). B5 dates it Monday 13 October; see UNVERIFIED. Bérubé: "Presque tout l'est du Canada était en pénurie" (outlet paraphrase) and "La demande est tellement forte pour ces produits [...] qu'on n'avait pas d'inventaire." Also Bérubé: "des emplois vont se sécuriser pour le long terme si l'investissement va de l'avant pour la crème sure et le cottage, on voudrait en faire un centre d'excellence." | B7 (Nov 21, 2025) |
| 10 Dec 2025 | Agropur is adding processing equipment at Amqui "qui permettra de produire plus de fromage cottage et de crème sûre", plus storage. VP Mylène Dupéré: "Ce que je peux vous dire, c'est qu'on parle de plusieurs millions de dollars." Production "pourrait s'intensifier dès 2027". The cut cheese-line jobs "n'allaient pas être rétablis". | B8 (Dec 10, 2025) |
| 3 Mar 2026 | CTV: "Agropur, makers of Sealtest cottage cheese, told CTV News their plant is operating at full capacity and that they have plans to increase production." | B3 (Mar 3, 2026) |
| 19 Sep 2026 | Agropur VP marketing and innovation, fresh products, Mariline Mailhot. The outlet paraphrases: "Mme Mailhot affirme que les ventes ont augmenté de plus de 60 % au cours des deux dernières années". Her quotes: "En ce moment, on n'est pas capable de répondre à cette demande." and "On veut vraiment faire de l'usine d'Amqui notre centre d'excellence au niveau de la production de fromage cottage." The outlet puts the Amqui investment at "plusieurs dizaines de millions de dollars". This contrasts with "plusieurs millions" in B8 (Dec 2025); see UNVERIFIED. | B9 (Sep 19, 2026) |

**Agropur press releases 2023–2026 relevant to the footprint (A16–A19)**
- **April 23, 2026 (A18).** Investment projects at Beauceville (QC) and Bedford (NS). CEO Émile Cordeau: "This strategic initiative, representing investments nearing $1 billion, involves replacing end-of-life equipment with innovative technologies and automation, enabling us to achieve efficiency gains and increase our milk processing and valorization capacity… these projects will support the creation of more than 90 skilled regional jobs, with over 60 positions in Beauceville and nearly 30 in Bedford." The release adds: "These projects remain subject to final approval by the end of 2026." Also: "$20 million in its Miramichi, New Brunswick plant", with NB support of "up to $2.4 million". The release's product framing is protein-worded and is not reproduced (house rule 1). These are dairy-ingredient projects, not a cottage cheese announcement.
- **July 15, 2026 (A17).** Sale of the fine cheese business to Lactalis: "The transaction includes namely the Oka and Saint‑Hyacinthe plants, as well as brands associated with this product category." President Roger Massicotte: "Although profitable, the fine cheese business represents only a small portion of our operations, accounting for approximately 2% of our consolidated revenue and 2% of the milk processed in our Canadian plants." Cordeau: "Over the coming years, Agropur will focus its efforts and investments on strategic activities that generate the greatest value for its dairy farmer members, namely the production of fluid milk, including value-added milk, industrial cheese, butter and dairy ingredients." The release says: "This transaction remains subject to approval by regulatory authorities and to customary, applicable closing conditions."
- **February 11, 2026 (A19).** 2025 results: "revenues of $8.9 billion for the 2025 fiscal year, representing 2.0% growth"; EBITDA "$686.4 million". Cordeau's quote about increasing "capacity to process value‑added products" is protein-framed; it is not reproduced.
- There is **no Agropur press release in the company's list about the Amqui lock-out, the Amqui cheese-line removal or the Amqui cottage cheese investment.** The press-release list shows nothing between 12 Feb 2025 and 11 Feb 2026 (A16). The Amqui items above rest on Radio-Canada, CTV and Le Saint-François (tier b).

### 5b. Gay Lea Foods Co-operative (Nordica)

- **August 11, 2026 (A21, company release).** "Gay Lea Foods Co-operative Limited ("Gay Lea Foods") today announced an investment of more than $200 million to significantly expand its Clayson Road dairy manufacturing facility in Toronto." Sub-headline: "Major facility expansion will increase availability of cottage cheese for Canadian consumers." Other verbatim lines:
  - "This investment will help address the national cottage cheese shortage…" The sentence continues with protein framing, which is trimmed.
  - "The project marks the first major milestone in Gay Lea Foods' approximately $450-million Network for Growth strategy…"
  - "The expansion, to be complete in 2028, will create up to 75 new positions at the Clayson facility…"
  - CEO Suzanna Dalrymple: "This investment reflects our confidence in the future of Canadian dairy and in Gay Lea Foods' role in helping shape it… That future will be built on Canadian dairy farms and in modern processing facilities equipped to respond to evolving consumer preferences."
  - Chair Andrew Henderson: "Expanding a facility that transforms Canadian milk into a product sold exclusively to Canadian consumers, is a natural extension of our co-operative's legacy."
  - Boilerplate: "owned by approximately 1,200 dairy farmer members in Ontario and Manitoba. Founded in 1958… brands including Gay Lea, Nordica, Salerno, Ivanhoe and Bothwell Cheese."
  - The release's fourth paragraph is health-framed; it is barred and not used (see Appendix A).
- **Annual Report 2025 (A22).** "Nordica Cottage Cheese was the standout performer, fueled by the "Just Add Nordica" campaign and expanded distribution in Western Canada." Also: "With the road map complete, implementation will begin in earnest in fiscal 2026," referring to Network for Growth (N4G). The report notes: "*Financial statements and any information considered proprietary have been removed…"
- **Company spokespeople in the press (tier b).**
  - Dalrymple to Global News (B4, Aug 13, 2026): "Our customers are asking for more. Unfortunately, we're sort of tapped out at our current capacities as we speak."
  - A Gay Lea spokesperson to CBC (B1, May 7, 2026): "What was once considered a niche product is now one of the fastest-growing segments in Canadian grocery stores." The CBC paraphrase adds that "Gay Lea has invested to meet growing demand, and 'in the meantime, availability may vary by region and retailer.'" The spokesperson's preceding sentence is health-framed and is not reproduced.
  - Spokesperson Robin Redstone to CBC Windsor (B2, May 25, 2026): "We have already invested significantly at Gay Lea Foods to increase production across our entire Nordica cottage cheese line … working with our retail partners and looking for ways to meet the demand." And: "The more of it that we can make, the better it is for our dairy farmers and the dairy industry in Canada."
- **Acquisitions and divestitures 2023–2026.** The Gay Lea news list (A23) shows no acquisition in 2026 (items: Aug 11, Jun 25, Apr 22, Apr 21, 2026). Just Food (B10, Aug 12, 2026) reports: "In May last year, Gay Lea Foods sold Notre Dame Creamery to local peer Fromagerie Chaeban. It took place three years after the co-operative bought Notre Dame Creamery through its 2022 acquisition of Bothwell Cheese." This is outlet-reported; no Gay Lea release was opened, so see UNVERIFIED. **No cottage cheese plant acquisition by Gay Lea in 2023–2026 was found.**

### 5c. Lactalis Canada (subsidiary of France's Lactalis Group)

- **July 15, 2026 (A24).** Lactalis "reached a definitive agreement with Agropur Cooperative to acquire assets of its fine cheese division including renowned Quebec-made brands OKA, Monsieur Gustav and L'Extra, two production facilities as well as its fine cheese import activities. The acquisition is subject to customary closing conditions and approval by Competition Bureau Canada. Financial terms of the agreement were not disclosed." Also: "Lactalis Canada will acquire two production facilities in Oka and Saint-Hyacinthe, Quebec and add approximately 400 employees to its 4,500 team across Canada." The company claims: "More than $900 million in capital investments and transformation projects… across 19 Canadian manufacturing sites", "Processing approximately 2.2 billion litres of 100% Canadian milk", and "the $1.62 billion acquisition of Kraft Heinz's natural cheese business in Canada". The company says so. **Label:** a proposed transaction pending Competition Bureau review. There is no Bureau decision on record here.
- **April 9, 2026 (A25).** Winchester, Ont. milk-receiving bay: "$16.4 million investment"; "Ability to receive up to two million litres of milk per day – an increase of approximately 25%"; "In 2026 alone, the company is investing $42 million at the site". The company calls Winchester "the largest cheese plant in Canada by volume".
- **March 30, 2026 (A26 list).** Granby, Que. yogurt plant event; the list text says "$60 million investments made at the site over the last five years".
- **Closure (tier b, B11, July 18, 2025).** The company, in an email statement quoted by Sudbury.com: "Lactalis Canada has made the difficult decision to close its plant-based operations in Sudbury, Ontario effective December 12, 2025, which will mark our full exit from the plant-based beverage category." That was a plant-based beverage plant, not dairy or cottage cheese.
- **Cottage cheese:** none of the Lactalis Canada releases opened mention cottage cheese. No Lactalis Canada cottage cheese product was found in its own brand list (A24 boilerplate). Do not describe Lactalis as a cottage cheese maker without a product page.

### 5d. Saputo Inc. (Dairyland, Neilson, Baxter, Scotsburn)

- "Saputo also produces, markets and distributes in Canada fluid milk, cream, yogurt, sour cream, cottage cheese and ice cream mixes" (A27). The brands listed include Baxter, Dairyland, Neilson and Scotsburn. The page gives no Canadian plant or employee count.
- AAFC (A4): Saputo "has 6,000 employees in Canada". AAFC also says: "The three players [Agropur, Lactalis Canada, Saputo] rank among the top 25 dairy processors globally."
- FY2026, year ended March 31, 2026 (A28): revenues $17,551 million from continuing operations; the Dairy Division (Argentina) is classified as discontinued. CEO Carl Colizza: "As we progress through the realization phase of the capital program we initiated in 2021, we are seeing tangible benefits in our cost structure, network capabilities, and overall performance consistency."
- Aug 14, 2026 (A30, release title as listed): "Saputo Enters Agreement to Sell its United Kingdom Operations". WF summary: to "B.S.A. SAS (Lactalis)". This is **U.K., not Canada**.
- Sept 23, 2026 (A29): Dave Paradis was named "President and Chief Operating Officer, Dairy Division (Canada)". "Mr. Paradis will lead Saputo's Canadian operations…" (as returned by WF).
- **Plant closures or openings in Canada, 2023–2026: none found.** Saputo's newsroom (newsroom.saputo.com) returned 403 to curl and timed out in WF. The Canadian closure releases that searches surfaced are dated 2014–2020 (for example CTV/CP "Saputo closing plants in Quebec, Ontario and Nova Scotia", published March 22, 2016: https://www.ctvnews.ca/montreal/article/saputo-closing-plants-in-quebec-ontario-and-nova-scotia/). Saputo's 2024–2025 closures that searches surfaced are **U.S.** plants. See UNVERIFIED.

### 5e. Other supply-side items (tier b only)

- Organic Meadow, a Canadian dairy producer, told CBC (B1) that it "had to discontinue its cottage cheese last September due to 'capacity constraints.'" Company quote: "We are actively exploring all options to address this situation and restore cottage cheese to our lineup." Per the article date, "last September" means September 2025.

### 7b. Grocery Code of Conduct

- The Bureau (A37, as returned): "The Bureau also made the commitment to provide a procompetitive perspective to support the implementation of Canada's grocery code of conduct."
- The Canadian Press via CBC (B15, Jan 1, 2026): "Starting Jan. 1, the code — which was being rolled out in stages — will be fully operational, including the dispute resolution mechanism…" "Canada's five largest grocers — Empire, Loblaw, Metro, Walmart Canada and Costco Canada — have now formally registered." Karen Proud, president and adjudicator of the Office of the Grocery Sector Code of Conduct: "It would be great if we had a [silver bullet] that could fix food inflation. It's really not the purpose of the code." And: "The code is aimed at improving the relationships between suppliers and retailers." CBC adds that the code "does not regulate pricing, control shelf placement or limit companies' ability to negotiate commercial terms."
- The Office's own site (grocerycode.ca) returned a 502 at CONNECT; it was not opened. See UNVERIFIED.

## 8. The U.S.–Canada dairy dispute, 2025–2026 (every U.S. statement is a U.S. CLAIM)

### 8a. 2025

| Date | U.S. statement (U.S. CLAIM), verbatim | Source | Canada / context |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~7–12 Mar 2025 | CBC reports that Trump said Canada charges "more than 200 per cent tariffs on dairy products" and made "an important omission"; CBC says he "mischaracterized" CUSMA. CBC paraphrase; no Trump verbatim opened | B12 (Mar 14, 2025) | CBC: "High Canadian tariffs only apply if the agreed tariff-rate quotas on U.S. dairy imports under CUSMA are reached or exceeded." CBC quotes IDFA's March 7 statement that the U.S. "has never gotten close to exceeding" the quotas |
| 27 Jun 2025 | Truth Social, as quoted by CBC: "We have just been informed that Canada, a very difficult Country to TRADE with, including the fact that they have charged our Farmers as much as 400% Tariffs, for years, on Dairy Products, has just announced that they are putting a Digital Services Tax on our American Technology Companies, which is a direct and blatant attack on our Country." | B13 (Jun 27, 2025) | CBC: "As he has done in the past, Trump mischaracterized Canada's tariff regime on U.S. dairy products. The high tariff rates Trump frequently cites are only applied if U.S. exports exceed a set 'tariff-rate quota,' something that has never happened." Foreign Affairs Minister Anita Anand called supply management a "cornerstone" Canadian economic policy that is "extremely important" |
| 26 Jun 2025 | (Canada) Bill C-202 received Royal Assent: no future TRQ increase or over-quota tariff cut for dairy, poultry or eggs in trade deals | A15, A14 | Canadian law |

### 8b. 2026: the Section 338 actions on cheese TRQs

| Date | U.S. action / U.S. CLAIM (verbatim) | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 20 Jul 2026 | Proclamation 11047 (dairy). "While Canada's eligibility criteria for the USMCA dairy TRQs — and specifically, the cheeses of all types TRQ — do not allow retailers to obtain and use TRQ quantities, the eligibility criteria for the CETA do grant retailers access to the TRQ quantity for cheese of all types." "I find as a fact that Canada is discriminating in fact against the commerce of the United States through Canada's TRQ allocation measures imposed on U.S. cheeses of all types under the USMCA…" It imposes "an additional ad valorem duty of 50 percent on certain products of Canada, as identified in Annex II… effective as of 12:01 a.m. eastern time on August 19, 2026." **Label: U.S. executive finding under U.S. law (U.S. claim). Not a trade-tribunal finding.** | A32 |
| 20 Jul 2026 | Fact sheet: "As part of its complicated and protectionist dairy system, Canada established tariff-rate quotas on U.S. cheese that are much more restrictive than the tariff-rate quotas imposed on similar imports of cheese into Canada from the EU, despite Canada having trade agreements with both the U.S. and the EU." Also: "These Section 338 tariffs apply to all covered goods regardless of whether a good originates under the U.S.-Mexico-Canada Agreement (USMCA)." | A31 |
| 18 Aug 2026 | Proclamation 11056: "According to senior executive branch officials, Canada has expressed a commitment to remove the discriminations…" The effective date moved to "12:01 a.m. eastern time on August 22, 2026." | A33 |
| 25 Aug 2026 | (Canada) Finance Canada: "Effective September 8, Canada will impose counter-tariffs of 15, 25 and 50 per cent on products drawn from those targeted by U.S. Section 338 and Section 232 tariffs, with the rate for each product matching the corresponding U.S. rate." "Goods subject to 25 per cent tariffs include appliances, dairy products, such as cheese, and certain steel and aluminum derivative products." Minister François-Philippe Champagne: "When the United States asked too much and offered too little, we chose to stand up for Canadians. Our dollar-for-dollar, rate for rate counter-tariffs as well as a multi-billion dollar support package will protect workers, farmers, families, and businesses…" (as returned by WF) | A36 |
| 8 Sep 2026 | Exclusion proclamation (dairy), U.S. CLAIM: "On August 21, 2026, Canada reneged on its commitment, ceased negotiating in good faith, and did not remove the discrimination…" "…certain products of Canada, as set forth in the Annex to this proclamation, are excluded from importation into the United States, effective with respect to goods imported on or after 12:01 a.m. eastern time on September 29, 2026." | A34 |
| 8 Sep 2026 | Fact sheet, U.S. CLAIM: "After breaking off trade talks with the United States last month, today Canada imposed new retaliatory tariffs on about $20 billion of U.S. exports, including steel, dairy, and agricultural equipment." The bans take effect "September 29, 2026" and product additions "September 15, 2026". It also says the July actions were modified by "replacing those products with new ones, ranging from all-terrain vehicles (ATVs) to additional dairy products." | A35 |
| 29 Sep 2026 | CBC: the U.S. ban list includes "Whey and whey products". "Various cheese products were added to a list of products subject to a 50 per cent tariff, but not banned outright." Scotiabank's Derek Holt: "These actions are face-saving by the U.S. administration, not substantive in nature and that's a positive." | B14 (Sep 29, 2026) |

- Figures to watch: the White House says Canada's counter-tariffs cover "about $20 billion" (A35). Finance Canada and outlets describe U.S. tariffs on "$27.6 billion" of Canadian goods. These are different measures from different parties; do not merge them.
- U.S. outlet (B16, Capital Press, July 21, 2026, **U.S. CONTEXT**): USDEC CEO Gregg Doud: "Canada simply cannot continue to discriminate against U.S. dairy farmers by effectively blocking negotiated access to its market." Carney "said the 50% tariffs violated the trade agreement". That is Capital Press's paraphrase of Carney.

The survey was self-commissioned and paid for by ARI." | Q8 stem: "Suppose making concessions in these areas helped Canada reach an agreement that removed or reduced U.S. tariffs. Would you support or oppose Canada making each of the following concessions?" Item: "Allowing more American dairy products into the Canadian market" | "opening the Canadian market to U.S. dairy (61% oppose, 28% support)" |

## Appendix A. Do not use on air (food-safety, recall and health-framed items)

- **Recall (food safety), do not use:** Agropur, "Agropur initiates a voluntary recall of certain milk products manufactured at its Bedford, Nova Scotia plant" (March 25, 2026), listed at https://www.agropur.com/en/news/press-releases. It concerns milk products, not cottage cheese. Listed only because it appears in the press-release record. The body was not opened.
- **Food-safety rationale in the Amqui lock-out, do not use:** Radio-Canada (B5) reports Agropur's statement that an intermittent strike "comporte des risques pour la salubrité, la sécurité alimentaire". This is a food-safety claim; barred on air. The labour-dispute facts in 5a can be used without it.
- **Agropur news item, do not use:** "Advancing Food Safety: Agropur experts contribute to industry-leading guidance" (Aug 26, 2026), listed at https://www.agropur.com/en/news.
- **U.S. CONTEXT recall, do not use:** the Great Value (U.S.) cottage cheese item mentioned in a U.S. YouTube transcript (scratchpad/tr_CE_cottage_232K.txt). Not opened, U.S., and barred.
- **Health and protein-framed sentences in sources, do not reproduce:**
  - CBC B1/B2 passages on "protein-rich", "diet food" and "health benefits", including the McMaster kinesiology professor's comments.
  - The CTV B3 dietitian's comments and a shopper's medical remark.
  - Global News B4's grams-of-protein paragraph.
  - Gay Lea's release paragraph on "nutritious, affordable health and wellness options" and its "high-protein" portfolio wording.
  - The protein wording in Agropur's April 23, 2026 and Feb 11, 2026 releases.
  - Le Saint-François B9's paragraph quoting an Agropur engineer on amino acids.
  - CBC B2's "protein-maxxing" explanation.
